PDF

Merge, split, organise, compress, watermark, number, sign and fill PDFs; text and images out.

pdf_compressCompress PDF

Shrink a PDF by re-rendering every page as a JPEG image (best for scans and photo-heavy files). Presets like the website: email (96 DPI, quality 55), balanced (144 DPI, 72), high (220 DPI, 85); or set dpi/quality yourself; optional grayscale. The result loses selectable text, links and form fields. Reports before/after size and a verdict ("bigger" means keep the original: the PDF was already efficient). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-compress/

InputTypeDescription
file requiredstringThe PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
preset"email" | "balanced" | "high"
default "balanced"		Quality preset. Default "balanced".
dpiintegerOverride the preset resolution (36-300 DPI).
qualityintegerOverride the preset JPEG quality (30-95).
grayscaleboolean
default false		Convert pages to grayscale.
passwordstringPassword, if the PDF is protected.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example: small enough to attach to an email

{
  "file": "scan.pdf",
  "preset": "email"
}
pdf_extract_pagesExtract PDF pages

Make one new PDF from selected pages of a PDF, e.g. "1-3, 7, 10-" (order as written, each page once). The rest of the file is left out. To reorder, duplicate or delete pages use pdf_organize. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-split/

InputTypeDescription
file requiredstringThe PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
pages requiredstringPages to keep: "1-3, 7, 10-" ("5-" = to the end; also "odd", "even", "last").
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example: writes report-pages-1-3_7.pdf

{
  "file": "report.pdf",
  "pages": "1-3, 7"
}
pdf_form_fieldsList PDF form fields

List the fillable fields of a PDF form in reading order: name, readable label, type (text, checkbox, radio, dropdown, list, signature, button), page, current value, options, and whether it is required / read-only / multi-line. Use the names with pdf_form_fill. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-fill/

InputTypeDescription
file requiredstringThe PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).

Example

{
  "file": "application.pdf"
}
pdf_form_fillFill a PDF form

Fill PDF form fields by name (get names from pdf_form_fields): text as strings, checkboxes as true/false, radio groups and dropdowns by option text, lists as arrays. Optionally flatten (bake the answers into the page, no longer editable). Only changed fields are redrawn. Text the standard font cannot draw (Arabic…) is kept and viewers are asked to draw it, but cannot be flattened. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-fill/

InputTypeDescription
file requiredstringThe PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
values requiredobjectField name -> value, e.g. {"name": "Sara", "agree": true, "plan": "pro", "langs": ["Arabic"]}.
flattenboolean
default false		Flatten the form after filling (fields become plain page content).
ignoreUnknownboolean
default false		Skip names that are not in the form instead of failing.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example

{
  "file": "application.pdf",
  "values": {
    "name": "Sara Haddad",
    "agree": true,
    "plan": "pro",
    "country": "Egypt"
  },
  "flatten": true
}
pdf_from_imagesImages to PDF

Make a PDF from images (JPEG, PNG, WebP, GIF, TIFF, AVIF, SVG…), one image per page, in the order given. Page size A4, Letter, or "fit" (page = image size at 96 dpi); orientation auto/portrait/landscape; margin in mm; quality "original" (JPEG/PNG kept as-is), high (≤3000 px, JPEG 90), medium (≤2000 px, 75) or small (≤1400 px, 60). Photos are turned upright from their EXIF orientation. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/images-to-pdf/

InputTypeDescription
images required(string | object)[]Images in page order: path/base64 strings, or {file, rotate}.
pageSize"a4" | "letter" | "fit"
default "a4"		Page size. Default "a4".
orientation"auto" | "portrait" | "landscape"
default "auto"		"auto" turns the page to match each image. Ignored for "fit".
marginnumber
default 10		Margin around each image in mm. Default 10.
quality"original" | "high" | "medium" | "small"
default "high"		Image quality / size trade-off. Default "high".
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example

{
  "images": [
    "receipt1.jpg",
    "receipt2.jpg"
  ],
  "output": "receipts.pdf"
}

Example

{
  "images": [
    "scan.png",
    {
      "file": "photo.jpg",
      "rotate": 90
    }
  ],
  "pageSize": "fit",
  "margin": 0,
  "quality": "original"
}
pdf_infoPDF info

Quick facts about a PDF for planning the next step: page count, page sizes (points, mm, paper name such as "A4 portrait", grouped by size), rotated pages, encryption, PDF version, form field count, title/author, and which pages have no text layer (scans that would need OCR). Related web tool: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-metadata/

InputTypeDescription
file requiredstringThe PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
passwordstringPassword, for the text check on a protected PDF.
textCheckboolean
default true		Check which pages have a text layer (reads up to 500 pages). Default true.

Example: { pages: 12, sizes: [{ paper: "A4 portrait", pages: "1-12" }], pagesWithoutText: [1, …] }

{
  "file": "scan.pdf"
}
pdf_mergeMerge PDFs

Combine several PDFs into one, in the order given. Each entry can be a file (path or base64) or {file, pages} to take only some pages ("1-3, 5, 8-"; "3-1" keeps reverse order; a page can repeat). Password-protected PDFs must be unlocked first. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-merge/

InputTypeDescription
files required(string | object)[]PDFs to merge, in order: a path/base64 string, or {file, pages}.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example: report + pages 2-4 of the appendix

{
  "files": [
    "report.pdf",
    {
      "file": "appendix.pdf",
      "pages": "2-4"
    }
  ],
  "output": "merged.pdf"
}
pdf_metadata_readRead PDF metadata

Show what a PDF reveals about itself: Title, Author, Subject, Keywords, Creator, Producer, creation/modification dates (with time zone), custom info fields, XMP metadata (size, edit history, creator) and the document ID. Optionally returns the XMP XML. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-metadata/

InputTypeDescription
file requiredstringThe PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
includeXmpboolean
default false		Also return the decoded XMP XML.

Example

{
  "file": "contract.pdf"
}
pdf_metadata_writeEdit or strip PDF metadata

Change PDF info fields (title, author, subject, keywords, creator, producer, dates, custom fields) or strip all metadata: info fields, XMP, page-level data and the document ID, and drop unreachable leftovers from earlier edits so no trace stays in the bytes. Untouched values keep their exact original form. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-metadata/

InputTypeDescription
file requiredstringThe PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
stripboolean
default false		Remove ALL metadata (other edits are ignored).
titlestringTitle. "" removes it; omit to keep it.
authorstringAuthor. "" removes it; omit to keep it.
subjectstringSubject. "" removes it; omit to keep it.
keywordsstringKeywords. "" removes it; omit to keep it.
creatorstringCreator (the app that made it). "" removes it; omit to keep it.
producerstringProducer (the PDF engine). "" removes it; omit to keep it.
creationDatestringCreation date as ISO 8601, e.g. "2026-03-14T09:30:00+03:00". "" removes it; omit to keep it.
modDatestringModification date as ISO 8601. "" removes it; omit to keep it.
customobjectCustom info fields to set; null removes one. E.g. {"Company": null}.
removeXmpboolean
default false		Remove the XMP metadata stream (and page-level XMP/PieceInfo).
removeDocumentIdboolean
default false		Remove the document ID from the trailer.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example

{
  "file": "report.pdf",
  "title": "Final report",
  "author": "Sara Haddad"
}

Example: writes cv-clean.pdf with no metadata

{
  "file": "cv.pdf",
  "strip": true
}
pdf_organizeOrganize PDF pages

Rebuild a PDF from a page list: reorder, delete (leave pages out), duplicate (list a page twice) and insert blank pages. "3, 1-2, blank, 5-" puts page 3 first, then 1-2, a blank page (same size as the page before it), then 5 to the end; page 4 is dropped. Optionally rotate pages of the result. Existing page rotation is kept. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-organizer/

InputTypeDescription
file requiredstringThe PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
pages requiredstringNew page order (1-based source pages): e.g. "3, 1-2, blank, 2, 5-". Pages not listed are removed; "blank" adds an empty page.
rotateobject[]Optional rotations applied to pages of the new order.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example: swap the first two pages, turn page 3

{
  "file": "scan.pdf",
  "pages": "2, 1, 3-",
  "rotate": [
    {
      "pages": "3",
      "angle": 90
    }
  ]
}
pdf_page_numbersAdd page numbers

Add page numbers to a PDF, plus an optional header and footer line. Format tokens: {n}, {total}, {date}, {filename} (e.g. "Page {n} of {total}", "{n} / {total}", "- {n} -"). Choose the corner/edge, start number, how many leading pages to skip (e.g. a cover), a page range, font, size, colour and margin. Numbers stay upright on rotated pages. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-page-numbers/

InputTypeDescription
file requiredstringThe PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
formatstring
default "Page {n} of {total}"		Number format with {n}, {total}, {date}, {filename}. Default "Page {n} of {total}". Empty = no number (header/footer only).
position"tl" | "tc" | "tr" | "bl" | "bc" | "br"
default "bc"		Where the number goes: t/b = top/bottom, l/c/r = left/centre/right. Default "bc".
startinteger
default 1		Number of the first numbered page. Default 1.
skipinteger
default 0		Leave this many first pages without a number (e.g. 1 for a cover). Default 0.
pagesstringPages to number: 1-based list like "1-3, 5, 8-" ("5-" = to the end, "-3" = first three, "3-1" = reversed). Also "all", "odd", "even", "last". Empty = all pages.
font"Helvetica" | "HelveticaBold" | "TimesRoman" | "Courier"
default "Helvetica"
sizenumber
default 10		Text size in points. Default 10.
colorstring
default "#333333"		Colour as hex "#rrggbb". Default #333333.
marginnumber
default 28		Distance from the page edge in points. Default 28.
headerstring
default ""		Optional header text on numbered pages (tokens allowed).
headerAlign"l" | "c" | "r"
default "c"		Header alignment: l, c or r.
footerstring
default ""		Optional footer text on numbered pages (tokens allowed).
footerAlign"l" | "c" | "r"
default "c"		Footer alignment: l, c or r.
datestringText for {date}. Default: today in the server locale.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example: no number on the cover

{
  "file": "report.pdf",
  "format": "Page {n} of {total}",
  "position": "br",
  "skip": 1
}

Example

{
  "file": "thesis.pdf",
  "format": "{n}",
  "footer": "Draft, {date}",
  "footerAlign": "l"
}
pdf_rotateRotate PDF pages

Rotate selected pages of a PDF by 90, 180 or 270 degrees clockwise (-90 = counter-clockwise), on top of any rotation they already have. Other pages, links and form fields are left as they are. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-organizer/

InputTypeDescription
file requiredstringThe PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
angle required90 | 180 | 270 | -90Clockwise degrees: 90, 180, 270, or -90.
pagesstringPages to rotate: 1-based list like "1-3, 5, 8-" ("5-" = to the end, "-3" = first three, "3-1" = reversed). Also "all", "odd", "even", "last". Empty = all pages.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example

{
  "file": "scan.pdf",
  "angle": 90,
  "pages": "even"
}
pdf_sign_imageSign PDF with an image

Place a signature (or initials, stamp, logo) image on PDF pages at x/y/width in points, measured from the top-left of the page as it is shown (rotated pages are handled). A light paper background is removed by default so scanned signatures sit cleanly on the page. Optional text marks (name, date…) can be added too. This is a visual signature, not a cryptographic digital signature. To draw a signature by hand, use the web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-sign/

InputTypeDescription
file requiredstringThe PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
signaturestringSignature image: local path or base64 (PNG, JPEG, WebP…).
pagesstring
default "last"		Pages to sign: "last" (default), "1", "all", "1-3"…
xnumber
default 72		Left edge in points from the left of the page (72 pt = 1 inch). Default 72.
ynumberTop edge in points from the TOP of the page. Default: 72 pt above the bottom margin.
widthnumber
default 150		Width in points. Default 150 (about 5.3 cm).
heightnumberHeight in points. Default: keeps the image aspect ratio.
removeBackgroundboolean
default true		Make light (paper) pixels transparent and trim to the ink. Default true.
thresholdinteger
default 210		Brightness (0-255) above which pixels become transparent. Default 210.
textsobject[]Optional text marks, e.g. the printed name or the date.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example

{
  "file": "contract.pdf",
  "signature": "signature.png",
  "pages": "last",
  "x": 360,
  "y": 700,
  "width": 160
}

Example

{
  "file": "form.pdf",
  "signature": "sig.jpg",
  "pages": "2",
  "x": 80,
  "y": 620,
  "texts": [
    {
      "text": "29/10/2026",
      "page": 2,
      "x": 300,
      "y": 640
    }
  ]
}
pdf_splitSplit PDF

Split one PDF into several PDFs written to a folder: by page ranges (one file per range, e.g. "1-3, 4-6, 7-"), every N pages, or one file per page. Files are named like the website: <name>-pages-1-3.pdf, <name>-part-01.pdf, <name>-page-001.pdf. Use pdf_extract_pages to get a single PDF with chosen pages. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-split/

InputTypeDescription
file requiredstringThe PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
mode required"ranges" | "every" | "single""ranges": one PDF per range in `ranges`; "every": a new PDF every `every` pages; "single": one PDF per page.
rangesstringFor mode "ranges": comma-separated ranges, each becomes one PDF, e.g. "1-3, 4-6, 7-".
everyintegerFor mode "every": pages per file (e.g. 2 makes 1-2, 3-4…).
outputstringFolder to write the PDFs into (created if missing). Default: a new folder in the server output folder.

Example: three PDFs

{
  "file": "book.pdf",
  "mode": "ranges",
  "ranges": "1-10, 11-20, 21-"
}

Example

{
  "file": "scans.pdf",
  "mode": "every",
  "every": 2
}
pdf_to_imagesPDF to images

Render PDF pages to PNG, JPEG or WebP images in a folder (one file per page, named <name>-p001.png…). Choose the DPI (72 = screen size, 150 = default, 300 = print), JPEG/WebP quality, a page range, and a transparent background for PNG/WebP. Very large pages are capped at 16384 px a side / 16.7 megapixels, like the website. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-to-images/

InputTypeDescription
file requiredstringThe PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
format"png" | "jpeg" | "webp"
default "png"		Image format. Default "png".
dpinumber
default 150		Resolution in dots per inch. Default 150.
qualityinteger
default 90		JPEG/WebP quality 1-100. Default 90.
transparentboolean
default false		Keep the page background transparent (PNG/WebP only).
pagesstringPages to render: 1-based list like "1-3, 5, 8-" ("5-" = to the end, "-3" = first three, "3-1" = reversed). Also "all", "odd", "even", "last". Empty = all pages.
passwordstringPassword, if the PDF is protected.
outputstringFolder to write the images into (created if missing). Default: a new folder in the server output folder.

Example

{
  "file": "slides.pdf",
  "format": "jpeg",
  "dpi": 96,
  "pages": "1-5"
}

Example

{
  "file": "logo.pdf",
  "format": "png",
  "dpi": 300,
  "transparent": true
}
pdf_to_textPDF to text

Extract the text of a PDF with lines and paragraphs rebuilt (right-to-left Arabic/Hebrew lines in reading order), as plain text or Markdown, with optional page separators and reflow (join lines, mend hyphenated words). Returns per-page word counts and flags pages with no text layer (scans: this tool does no OCR). Supports page ranges and passwords. Give "output" to write a .txt/.md file instead of returning the text inline. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-to-text/

InputTypeDescription
file requiredstringThe PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
pagesstringPages to read: 1-based list like "1-3, 5, 8-" ("5-" = to the end, "-3" = first three, "3-1" = reversed). Also "all", "odd", "even", "last". Empty = all pages.
passwordstringPassword, if the PDF is protected.
format"text" | "markdown"
default "text"		"text" or "markdown" (## Page N headings, reflowed paragraphs).
separatorsboolean
default true		Put a "——— Page N ———" line before each page. Default true.
reflowboolean
default false		Join the lines of each paragraph and mend words split by a hyphen.
perPageboolean
default false		Also return each page's text separately.
maxCharsinteger
default 200000		Cut the inline text after this many characters (the file output is never cut).
outputstringWrite the text to this file (or folder ending in "/") instead of returning it inline.

Example

{
  "file": "article.pdf",
  "reflow": true
}

Example

{
  "file": "book.pdf",
  "pages": "1-20",
  "format": "markdown",
  "output": "book.md"
}
pdf_watermarkWatermark PDF

Stamp a text or image watermark on PDF pages: centred or tiled across the page, with opacity, angle, size, colour and a page range. It reads upright on rotated pages. Text the standard PDF fonts cannot draw (Arabic, CJK…) is stamped as an image. Defaults match the website: "CONFIDENTIAL", Helvetica Bold 72 pt, #d64545, 25% opacity, 45°. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pdf-watermark/

InputTypeDescription
file requiredstringThe PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
textstringWatermark text (default "CONFIDENTIAL"). Ignored when "image" is given.
imagestringImage watermark instead of text: a local path or base64 (PNG, JPEG, WebP, SVG…).
font"HelveticaBold" | "Helvetica" | "TimesRomanBold" | "CourierBold"
default "HelveticaBold"		Font for text.
sizenumber
default 72		Text size in points. Default 72.
colorstring
default "#d64545"		Colour as hex "#rrggbb". Default #d64545.
scalenumber
default 40		Image width as % of the page width. Default 40.
opacitynumber
default 25		Opacity in % (1-100). Default 25.
anglenumber
default 45		Rotation in degrees, counter-clockwise as shown. Default 45.
mode"center" | "tile"
default "center"		"center": one stamp in the middle; "tile": repeated across the page.
gapnumber
default 80		Space between tiles in points (tile mode). Default 80.
pagesstringPages to stamp: 1-based list like "1-3, 5, 8-" ("5-" = to the end, "-3" = first three, "3-1" = reversed). Also "all", "odd", "even", "last". Empty = all pages.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example

{
  "file": "roadmap.pdf",
  "text": "DRAFT",
  "opacity": 20
}

Example

{
  "file": "brochure.pdf",
  "image": "logo.png",
  "mode": "tile",
  "scale": 15,
  "angle": 0,
  "opacity": 10
}

Images

Convert, resize, crop, compress, watermark, EXIF, palettes, favicons, placeholders.

image_ascii_artImage to ASCII art

Turn an image into ASCII art: each character cell takes the average brightness of its area (cells are twice as tall as wide) and maps it onto a character ramp: standard " .:-=+*#%@", detailed (70 characters), blocks " ░▒▓█", simple, binary, or your own. Contrast, brightness and invert (for light text on dark backgrounds). Returns the text; format "png" also renders it to an image (optionally in the image's colours). Transparent areas count as white paper. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/ascii-art/

InputTypeDescription
image requiredstringThe image: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
columnsinteger
default 100		Characters per line. Default 100.
ramp"standard" | "detailed" | "blocks" | "simple" | "binary" | "custom"
default "standard"		Character set, light → dark. Default standard.
customRampstring
default " .oO0@"		Characters for ramp "custom", from no ink to most ink (at least 2).
contrastnumber
default 0		-100 … 100. Default 0.
brightnessnumber
default 0		-100 … 100. Default 0.
invertboolean
default false		For light text on a dark background. Default false.
format"text" | "png"
default "text"		text (default) or png (also writes <name>-ascii.png).
colorboolean
default false		PNG only: colour each character with the image colour.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).

Example

{
  "image": "/photos/cat.jpg",
  "columns": 80,
  "contrast": 20
}

Example

{
  "image": "/photos/logo.png",
  "ramp": "blocks",
  "format": "png",
  "color": true
}
image_ascii_bannerText to ASCII banner

Big banner letters from text, using the website's own bitmap fonts: block (█ 5×7), char (any fill character, e.g. #), shadow (█ with ░ shade), compact (half-block 5×7), small (█ 3×5), tiny (half-block 3×5). Letters A-Z, digits and common punctuation (unknown characters show as "?"); one banner per input line. Returns the text; format "png" also renders it to banner.png. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/ascii-art/

InputTypeDescription
text requiredstringText to render (case-insensitive). New lines make separate banners.
style"block" | "char" | "shadow" | "compact" | "small" | "tiny"
default "block"		Letter style. Default block.
fillstring
default "#"		Fill character(s) for style "char". Default "#".
format"text" | "png"
default "text"		text (default) or png (also writes banner.png).
theme"dark" | "light"
default "dark"		PNG colours: dark (teal on near-black, default) or light.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).

Example: A 7-line banner: "## ## ######" …

{
  "text": "HI",
  "style": "char",
  "fill": "#"
}

Result

{
  "lines": 7
}
image_compressCompress image

Make an image file smaller: re-encode at a quality (default 80) and optionally cap the longest side (maxSide), as JPEG, WebP, AVIF or PNG ("keep" = same format; GIF/BMP/TIFF become PNG). Or give targetBytes to find the highest quality that fits that size. With keepSmaller (default on) the original is kept when re-encoding would not make it smaller. Reports before/after sizes and % saved. Transparent areas become white in JPEG. Metadata (EXIF, GPS) is not copied. PNG is lossless (quality ignored). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/image-squeeze/

InputTypeDescription
image requiredstringThe image: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
format"keep" | "jpeg" | "webp" | "avif" | "png"
default "keep"		Output format. Default keep (same as the input).
qualityinteger
default 80		Quality 1-100 for JPEG / WebP / AVIF. Default 80 (website default).
maxSideintegerScale down so the longest side is at most this many pixels (e.g. 1920).
targetBytesintegerAim for a file of at most this many bytes: searches the highest quality that fits (lossy formats only; overrides quality).
keepSmallerboolean
default true		Keep the original bytes when the result would be larger (same format and size only). Default true.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example

{
  "image": "/photos/IMG_0042.jpg",
  "quality": 75,
  "maxSide": 1920
}

Example: Highest WebP quality that fits 150 KB.

{
  "image": "/site/hero.png",
  "format": "webp",
  "targetBytes": 150000
}
image_convertConvert image format

Convert an image to PNG, JPEG, WebP, AVIF, GIF or TIFF. Reads JPEG, PNG, WebP, AVIF/HEIF, GIF (first frame), TIFF, SVG, BMP and ICO. Transparent areas are filled with `background` when writing JPEG (or always with `flatten`). EXIF rotation is applied. SVGs are rasterised at their own size (width/height or viewBox; 1024×1024 if neither), scripts never run. Images larger than 16384 px a side or 100 MP are scaled down to fit. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/image-convert/

InputTypeDescription
image requiredstringThe image: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
format"png" | "jpeg" | "webp" | "avif" | "gif" | "tiff"
default "png"		Output format. Default png (as on the website).
qualityinteger
default 92		Quality 1-100 for JPEG / WebP / AVIF (ignored for PNG). Default 92, same as the website.
backgroundstring
default "#ffffff"		Colour used for transparent areas when writing JPEG or when flatten is on. Default #ffffff.
flattenboolean
default false		Fill transparency with the background for every format, not only JPEG.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example: Writes logo.jpg with transparency on white.

{
  "image": "/photos/logo.png",
  "format": "jpeg",
  "quality": 90,
  "background": "#ffffff"
}

Example

{
  "image": "/icons/icon.svg",
  "format": "webp"
}

image_exif_readRead photo metadata (EXIF)

Show the hidden metadata in a photo: camera make/model, lens, date taken, exposure, ISO, software, author/copyright, serial numbers, comments, PNG text chunks, and GPS coordinates (where the photo was taken). image_exif_stripRemove photo metadata (EXIF)

Remove EXIF, GPS location, XMP, IPTC, comments, PNG text chunks and embedded previews from a photo before sharing it. JPEG and PNG are cleaned losslessly (image data copied byte for byte, like the website); by default the rotation is kept as a tiny orientation-only EXIF and the ICC colour profile is kept. applyOrientation re-encodes JPEGs with the rotation baked into the pixels. WebP/AVIF/HEIC/TIFF are re-encoded without metadata. Output name <name>-clean.<ext>. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/exif-cleaner/

InputTypeDescription
image requiredstringThe photo: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
keepOrientationboolean
default true		Keep only the Orientation tag so the photo still shows the right way up. Default true.
keepIccboolean
default true		Keep the ICC colour profile (no personal data; keeps colours right). Default true.
applyOrientationboolean
default false		Instead of keeping the Orientation tag, rotate the pixels (re-encodes JPEG at `quality`; PNG is unaffected). Default false.
qualityinteger
default 92		JPEG/WebP/AVIF quality when re-encoding is needed. Default 92.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example: Writes IMG_1234-clean.jpg: no GPS, camera or date; same pixels.

{
  "image": "/photos/IMG_1234.jpg"
}

Example

{
  "image": "/photos/IMG_1234.jpg",
  "applyOrientation": true
}

image_rotateRotate / flip image

Rotate an image clockwise by 90, 180, 270 or any angle (the canvas grows to fit; new corners get `background`), and/or flip it horizontally (mirror) or vertically. Flips apply after the rotation, as seen on screen. EXIF orientation is applied first (autoOrient, default on), so with angle 0 this simply "fixes" sideways phone photos. Output JPEG/PNG/WebP/AVIF ("keep" = same as input), name <name>-rotated.<ext>. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/photo-filters/

InputTypeDescription
image requiredstringThe image: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
anglenumber
default 90		Clockwise rotation in degrees (negative = counter-clockwise). Default 90.
flipHorizontalboolean
default false		Mirror left-right (after rotating).
flipVerticalboolean
default false		Mirror top-bottom (after rotating).
autoOrientboolean
default true		Apply the EXIF Orientation tag first (what browsers show). Default true.
backgroundstring
default "#00000000"		Fill for the corners uncovered by a non-right-angle rotation. Default transparent (white in JPEG).
format"keep" | "jpeg" | "png" | "webp" | "avif"
default "keep"		Output format. Default keep (JPEG/PNG/WebP stay, others become PNG).
qualityinteger
default 92		Quality 1-100 for JPEG / WebP / AVIF (ignored for PNG). Default 92, same as the website.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example

{
  "image": "/photos/scan.jpg",
  "angle": 90
}

Example: Un-mirror a selfie.

{
  "image": "/photos/selfie.jpg",
  "angle": 0,
  "flipHorizontal": true
}

Example: Bake the EXIF rotation into the pixels.

{
  "image": "/photos/IMG_1234.jpg",
  "angle": 0
}

image_to_base64Image to Base64 / data URI

Encode an image (or any file) as Base64: a data URI, bare Base64, a CSS background-image rule, an HTML <img> tag (with width/height), Markdown, or for SVG a URL-encoded data URI (usually ~30% smaller). Detects the type from the bytes and warns when the result is too big to inline (over 10 KB: slows pages; over 1 MB: use a normal file). Results longer than maxChars are written to a .txt file instead of being returned. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/image-to-base64/

InputTypeDescription
image requiredstringThe image: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
as"dataUri" | "base64" | "css" | "html" | "markdown" | "svgUri"
default "dataUri"		What to return: dataUri (default), base64, css, html, markdown, or svgUri (SVG only).
mimeTypestringOverride the detected MIME type (e.g. image/svg+xml).
maxCharsinteger
default 200000		Longest text returned inline; longer results go to a file. Default 200000.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).

Example: background-image: url("data:image/png;base64,…");

{
  "image": "/site/icons/logo.png",
  "as": "css"
}

Example

{
  "image": "/site/icons/arrow.svg",
  "as": "svgUri"
}
image_watermarkWatermark image

Add a text or logo watermark to a photo. Size is a % of the photo width, margin a % of the shorter side, so the result looks the same on any resolution. One of 9 positions or tiled across the whole image (brick pattern), with opacity, rotation, colour, weight and a soft shadow for text. Logo: any image (PNG with transparency works best). Output keeps the input format (JPEG/PNG/WebP) or as chosen; name <name>-watermarked.<ext>. Text uses the Inter font when installed, else a system sans-serif. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/watermark/

InputTypeDescription
image requiredstringThe photo: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
textstring
default "© Your Name"		Watermark text (ignored when a logo is given). Default "© Your Name".
logostringUse this image as the watermark instead of text: a local path or base64.
colorstring
default "#ffffff"		Text colour. Default #ffffff.
weight"400" | "500" | "600" | "700" | "800" | "900"
default "700"		Font weight. Default 700.
shadowboolean
default true		Soft drop shadow behind text. Default true.
position"top-left" | "top" | "top-right" | "left" | "center" | "right" | "bottom-left" | "bottom" | "bottom-right"
default "bottom-right"		Where the watermark sits (ignored when tiled). Default bottom-right.
tiledboolean
default false		Repeat the watermark across the whole photo.
opacitynumber
default 60		Opacity in %. Default 60.
sizenumber
default 28		Watermark width as % of the photo width. Default 28.
rotationnumber
default 0		Rotation in degrees (e.g. -30 for a diagonal tile). Default 0.
marginnumber
default 4		Margin from the edge in % of the shorter side; spacing between tiles when tiled. Default 4.
format"keep" | "jpeg" | "png" | "webp"
default "keep"		Output format. Default keep.
qualityinteger
default 92		Quality 1-100 for JPEG / WebP / AVIF (ignored for PNG). Default 92, same as the website.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example

{
  "image": "/photos/shoot-01.jpg",
  "text": "© Studio Lina",
  "position": "bottom-right",
  "opacity": 60
}

Example

{
  "image": "/photos/proof.jpg",
  "text": "PROOF",
  "tiled": true,
  "rotation": -25,
  "opacity": 35,
  "size": 26,
  "margin": 3
}

Example

{
  "image": "/photos/product.png",
  "logo": "/brand/logo.png",
  "position": "top-left",
  "size": 20
}

Text

Counting, case, slugs, cleaning, diffs, readability, Arabic tools, numbers to words, citations.

text_arabicArabic text tools

Arabic text utilities. mode "clean": remove tashkeel (harakat and Quranic marks, optionally keeping shadda), remove tatweel (kashida), normalise letters (alef with hamza/madda/wasla → bare alef; alef maqsura and Farsi ya → ya; ta marbuta → ha; hamza on waw/ya → waw/ya), switch Latin , ; ? to Arabic punctuation, and convert digits (Arabic-Indic ↔ Western). mode "arabizi": turn Arabizi / Franco-Arabic chat text (mar7aba, 3arabi, 5ala9, el bait) into Arabic script. Returns the result with letter/word/character counts. Give "text" or a text file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/arabic-tools/

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe text to transform.
pathstringOr: a path to a UTF-8 text file.
mode"clean" | "arabizi"
default "clean"		"clean" = clean & normalise Arabic text (default); "arabizi" = Arabizi (Latin letters + numbers) → Arabic script.
removeTashkeelboolean
default true		clean: remove tashkeel / harakat (default true).
keepShaddaboolean
default false		clean: when removing tashkeel, keep the shadda.
removeTatweelboolean
default true		clean: remove tatweel / kashida (default true).
normalizeAlefboolean
default false		clean: alef with hamza above/below, madda or wasla → bare alef.
normalizeYaboolean
default false		clean: alef maqsura and Farsi ya → Arabic ya.
taMarbutaToHaboolean
default false		clean: ta marbuta → ha.
normalizeHamzaboolean
default false		clean: hamza on waw → waw, hamza on ya → ya.
arabicPunctuationboolean
default false		clean: Latin comma, semicolon and question mark → Arabic ones.
digits"keep" | "western" | "arabic"
default "keep"		clean: keep digits, convert Arabic-Indic/Persian digits to 0-9 ("western"), or 0-9 to Arabic-Indic ("arabic").
outputstringOptional: write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/") instead of returning it.

Example

{
  "mode": "arabizi",
  "text": "mar7aba, kifak?"
}

Result

{
  "text": "مرحبا، كيفك؟",
  "arabicLetters": 9,
  "words": 2,
  "characters": 12
}

Example

{
  "text": "2026",
  "digits": "arabic"
}

Result

{
  "text": "٢٠٢٦",
  "arabicLetters": 0,
  "words": 1,
  "characters": 4,
  "marksRemoved": 0
}
text_caseCase convert

Convert text case: upper, lower, title (smart: keeps small words lower and iPhone/NASA as typed), sentence, camel, pascal, snake, kebab, constant, alternate, inverse. Works line by line and handles Unicode (Arabic, accents, ß). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/case-convert/

InputTypeDescription
text requiredstringText to convert.
mode required"upper" | "lower" | "title" | "sentence" | "camel" | "pascal" | "snake" | "kebab" | "constant" | "alternate" | "inverse"Target case.

Example

{
  "text": "a tale OF two cities",
  "mode": "title"
}

Result

{
  "result": "A Tale of Two Cities"
}

Example

{
  "text": "XMLParser handler",
  "mode": "snake"
}

Result

{
  "result": "xml_parser_handler"
}
text_citationCitation generator

Format references from structured fields in APA 7, MLA 9, Chicago author-date (17th), Harvard (Cite Them Right) or IEEE, for books, book chapters, journal articles, web pages, newspaper/magazine articles, videos and reports. Handles author-count rules (et al.), initials, organisations as authors, editions, page ranges (MLA 127-53), DOIs, URLs and access dates, and title case for MLA/Chicago/IEEE. Returns each citation as plain text and HTML (italics as <i>), the in-text citation, missing-field hints, and the whole list as a bibliography sorted for the style (numbered for IEEE). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/citation-gen/

InputTypeDescription
style required"apa" | "mla" | "chicago" | "harvard" | "ieee"Citation style: apa, mla, chicago, harvard or ieee.
sources requiredobject[]One or more sources.
autoCaseboolean
default true		Apply title case where the style wants it (MLA, Chicago, IEEE book titles). Default true.

Example: Abelson, H., & Sussman, G. J. (1996). Structure and interpretation of computer programs (2nd ed.). MIT Press.

{
  "style": "apa",
  "sources": [
    {
      "type": "book",
      "authors": [
        {
          "first": "Harold",
          "last": "Abelson"
        },
        {
          "first": "Gerald Jay",
          "last": "Sussman"
        }
      ],
      "title": "Structure and interpretation of computer programs",
      "edition": "2",
      "year": "1996",
      "publisher": "MIT Press",
      "place": "Cambridge, MA, USA"
    }
  ]
}

Result

{
  "style": "APA 7",
  "citations": [
    {
      "text": "Abelson, H., & Sussman, G. J. (1996). Structure and interpretation of computer programs (2nd ed.). MIT Press.",
      "html": "Abelson, H., &amp; Sussman, G. J. (1996). <i>Structure and interpretation of computer programs</i> (2nd ed.). MIT Press.",
      "inText": "(Abelson & Sussman, 1996)",
      "missing": []
    }
  ],
  "bibliographyTitle": "References",
  "bibliography": "Abelson, H., & Sussman, G. J. (1996). Structure and interpretation of computer programs (2nd ed.). MIT Press."
}
text_cleanText cleaner

Clean up text line by line with a fixed pipeline (same order as the website): strip HTML tags and decode entities → remove emojis → trim each line → collapse repeated spaces → remove empty lines → remove duplicate lines (optionally ignoring case) → sort (A–Z, Z–A, natural, by length) → reverse → shuffle (seedable) → add line numbers. Returns the cleaned text plus counts, or writes it to a file when "output" is given. Give "text" or a text file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/text-cleaner/

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe text to clean.
pathstringOr: a path to a UTF-8 text file.
stripHtmlboolean
default false		Strip HTML tags (drops script/style/comments, block ends become line breaks) and decode entities (default false).
removeEmojiboolean
default false		Remove emojis (©, ® and ™ are kept) (default false).
trimboolean
default true		Trim spaces at the start and end of each line (default true).
collapseSpacesboolean
default true		Collapse runs of spaces/tabs into one space (default true).
removeEmptyLinesboolean
default true		Remove empty (whitespace-only) lines (default true).
removeDuplicatesboolean
default false		Remove duplicate lines, keeping the first (default false).
ignoreCaseboolean
default false		When removing duplicates, treat lines that differ only in case as duplicates (default false).
sort"none" | "az" | "za" | "natural" | "length"
default "none"		Sort lines: none (default), az, za, natural (item 2 before item 10), length (shortest first).
reverseboolean
default false		Reverse the line order (default false).
shuffleboolean
default false		Shuffle the lines (default false).
seedintegerSeed for a reproducible shuffle (default: random).
numberLinesboolean
default false		Prefix each line with its number ("1. ", padded) (default false).
outputstringOptional: write the cleaned text to this file path (or folder ending in "/") instead of returning it.

Example

{
  "text": "item 10\nitem 2\nitem 2\n\nitem 1",
  "removeDuplicates": true,
  "sort": "natural"
}

Result

{
  "text": "item 1\nitem 2\nitem 10",
  "linesIn": 5,
  "linesOut": 3,
  "duplicatesRemoved": 1,
  "emptyLinesRemoved": 1,
  "charactersOut": 21
}
text_diffDiff two texts

Compare two texts line by line (Myers diff) and return a unified diff (3 lines of context, like `diff -u`), counts of added/removed/unchanged lines, and change blocks where each removed line is paired with the added line that replaced it and the changed words are marked [-old-] / {+new+}. Can ignore whitespace and case. Works with any language (Arabic included). Up to 50,000 lines per side. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/diff-view/

InputTypeDescription
originalstringThe original text ("before").
originalPathstringOr: path to the original text file.
changedstringThe changed text ("after").
changedPathstringOr: path to the changed text file.
ignoreWhitespaceboolean
default false		Treat lines that differ only in spacing as equal.
ignoreCaseboolean
default false		Treat lines that differ only in letter case as equal.
wordDiffboolean
default true		Mark the changed words inside paired lines (default true).
nameAstringLabel for the "---" header (default "original").
nameBstringLabel for the "+++" header (default "changed").

Example

{
  "original": "one\ntwo\nthree",
  "changed": "zero\none\nthree"
}

Result

{
  "identical": false,
  "added": 1,
  "removed": 1,
  "unchanged": 2,
  "unified": "--- original\n+++ changed\n@@ -1,3 +1,3 @@\n+zero\n one\n-two\n three\n",
  "changes": [
    {
      "removed": [],
      "added": [
        {
          "line": 1,
          "text": "zero"
        }
      ]
    },
    {
      "removed": [
        {
          "line": 2,
          "text": "two"
        }
      ],
      "added": []
    }
  ]
}

Example: changes[0]: removed marked "the [-quick-] fox", added marked "the {+slow+} fox"

{
  "original": "the quick fox",
  "changed": "the slow fox"
}

Result

{
  "identical": false,
  "added": 1,
  "removed": 1,
  "unchanged": 0,
  "unified": "--- original\n+++ changed\n@@ -1 +1 @@\n-the quick fox\n+the slow fox\n",
  "changes": [
    {
      "removed": [
        {
          "line": 1,
          "text": "the quick fox",
          "marked": "the [-quick-] fox"
        }
      ],
      "added": [
        {
          "line": 1,
          "text": "the slow fox",
          "marked": "the {+slow+} fox"
        }
      ]
    }
  ]
}
text_escapeEscape / unescape text

Escape or unescape a string for a target: JSON (string body), JavaScript (incl. template literals), HTML, XML, URL (encodeURIComponent, optional form +), SQL (doubled quotes, optional MySQL backslashes and wrapping quotes), CSV field (RFC 4180; comma, semicolon or tab), regex, POSIX shell single quotes, C/C++, Java/C#, and Unicode escapes (\uXXXX, \u{X}, \UXXXXXXXX, &#xX;, U+XXXX). Options per target as on the website. Set "allTargets" to get the escaped form for every target at once. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/text-escape/

InputTypeDescription
text requiredstringThe text to escape, or the escaped text to unescape.
target required"json" | "js" | "html" | "xml" | "url" | "sql" | "csv" | "regex" | "shell" | "c" | "java" | "unicode"Format: json, js, html, xml, url, sql, csv, regex, shell, c, java, unicode.
direction"escape" | "unescape"
default "escape"		escape (default) or unescape.
asciiboolean
default false		json/js/html/c/java: also escape non-ASCII characters (c: as UTF-8 octal).
namedboolean
default false		html (with ascii): prefer named entities such as &copy;.
formboolean
default false		url: form style, + for spaces.
wrapboolean
default false		sql: wrap in single quotes (unescape: strip them).
mysqlboolean
default false		sql: escape backslashes too (MySQL).
alwaysQuoteboolean
default false		csv: always quote the field.
delimiter"," | ";" | "\t"
default ","		csv: field delimiter (comma, semicolon or tab).
unicodeFormat"u4" | "ubrace" | "u8" | "html" | "uplus"
default "u4"		unicode: u4 = \uXXXX (JS, JSON, Java), ubrace = \u{X} (ES6, Rust, Swift), u8 = \UXXXXXXXX (Python, C), html = &#xX;, uplus = U+XXXX.
everyboolean
default false		unicode: escape every character, not only non-ASCII.
allTargetsboolean
default false		escape only: also return the text escaped for every target.

Example

{
  "text": "O'Brien",
  "target": "sql",
  "wrap": true
}

Result

{
  "result": "'O''Brien'",
  "note": "Standard SQL doubles single quotes. Prefer parameterised queries over escaping.",
  "inputCharacters": 7,
  "outputCharacters": 10
}

Example

{
  "text": "a%20b%26c",
  "target": "url",
  "direction": "unescape"
}

Result

{
  "result": "a b&c",
  "note": "encodeURIComponent: for one query value or path segment.",
  "inputCharacters": 9,
  "outputCharacters": 5
}
text_fancyFancy text (Unicode styles)

Restyle text with Unicode look-alike characters for bios and posts (28 styles): Bold, Italic, Bold italic, Script, Bold script, Fraktur, Bold fraktur, Double-struck, Sans-serif, Sans bold, Sans italic, Sans bold italic, Monospace, Circled, Circled dark, Squared, Squared dark, Parenthesized, Fullwidth, Small caps, Superscript, Upside down, Strikethrough, Underline, Double underline, Slashed, Overline, Wide spaced. Only Latin letters and digits change (other scripts such as Arabic and emoji pass through); some styles cover only capitals or only some symbols. Give one "style", or none to get every style. Up to 20,000 characters. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/fancy-text/

InputTypeDescription
text requiredstringThe text to restyle.
style"Bold" | "Italic" | "Bold italic" | "Script" | "Bold script" | "Fraktur" | "Bold fraktur" | "Double-struck" | "Sans-serif" | "Sans bold" | "Sans italic" | "Sans bold italic" | "Monospace" | "Circled" | "Circled dark" | "Squared" | "Squared dark" | "Parenthesized" | "Fullwidth" | "Small caps" | "Superscript" | "Upside down" | "Strikethrough" | "Underline" | "Double underline" | "Slashed" | "Overline" | "Wide spaced"One style name (exact, e.g. "Bold", "Script", "Upside down"). Omit to get all styles.

Example

{
  "text": "Hello 2026",
  "style": "Bold"
}

Result

{
  "results": [
    {
      "style": "Bold",
      "text": "𝐇𝐞𝐥𝐥𝐨 𝟐𝟎𝟐𝟔"
    }
  ]
}

Example: Returns all 28 styles.

  "text": "Stay curious"
}

text_layout_fixKeyboard layout fixer (EN ⇄ AR)

Fix text typed with the wrong keyboard layout between US English (QWERTY) and Arabic (101): "hgsghl ugd;l" becomes the Arabic greeting, and Arabic gibberish typed on English keys becomes "hello". Direction "auto" detects it; in mixed text only the words that look mistyped are converted (using small English/Arabic letter-pair models), so words that are already right stay as they are. Handles the shift layer (harakat, hamza forms), lam-alef keys and Caps Lock (all-caps Latin input is treated as lowercase). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/layout-fixer/

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe mistyped text.
pathstringOr: a path to a UTF-8 text file.
direction"auto" | "ar" | "en"
default "auto"		"auto" (default) detects; "ar" = typed on English keys, convert to Arabic; "en" = typed on Arabic keys, convert to English.
capsLockboolean
default false		Caps Lock was on: treat capital Latin letters as lowercase (detected automatically when all letters are capitals).

Example

{
  "text": ";dt phg;?"
}

Result

{
  "text": "كيف حالك؟",
  "direction": "ar",
  "wordsChanged": 2,
  "capsLockIgnored": false
}

Example: Mixed text: only the mistyped Arabic-looking word becomes "hello".

{
  "text": "Hi, ok اثممخ"
}

Result

{
  "text": "Hi, ok hello",
  "direction": "mixed",
  "wordsChanged": 1,
  "capsLockIgnored": false
}
text_loremLorem ipsum generator

Generate placeholder text: classic Lorem ipsum, readable English filler, or Arabic filler. Choose paragraphs (max 100), sentences (max 500) or words (max 10000); optionally start with the classic opener and wrap paragraphs in <p> tags. Give a seed for repeatable output (the same seed gives the same text). Writes a .txt/.html file instead when "output" is given. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/lorem-gen/

InputTypeDescription
style"lorem" | "english" | "arabic"
default "lorem"		Word list: lorem (Latin), english, or arabic.
unit"paragraphs" | "sentences" | "words"
default "paragraphs"		What "count" counts.
countinteger
default 3		How many paragraphs / sentences / words (clamped to the unit limit).
openerboolean
default true		Start with the classic opening line ("Lorem ipsum dolor sit amet…", "The quick brown fox…", or the Arabic one). Default true.
htmlboolean
default false		Wrap each paragraph in <p>…</p>.
seedintegerSeed for repeatable output (default: random).
outputstringOptional: write the text to this file path (or folder ending in "/") instead of returning it.

Example: 25 words starting "Lorem ipsum dolor sit amet…"

{
  "unit": "words",
  "count": 25,
  "seed": 42
}

Example

{
  "style": "arabic",
  "unit": "paragraphs",
  "count": 2
}
text_morseMorse code translator

Translate text to International Morse code and back. Encoding: letters separated by spaces, words by " / "; accents are folded (é → E) and characters without a code are skipped and counted. Decoding accepts . and - plus look-alikes (• · – — _), with words split by /, | or 2+ spaces; unknown codes become #. Encoding also returns the playing time at a given speed (PARIS timing: dot = 1200/WPM ms). No audio. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/morse-code/

InputTypeDescription
text requiredstringText to encode, or Morse code to decode.
mode"encode" | "decode" | "auto"
default "auto"		"encode" text → Morse, "decode" Morse → text, or "auto" (default): decode if the input only has Morse symbols, spaces, / and |.
wpmnumber
default 18		Speed in words per minute for the duration estimate (default 18).

Example

{
  "text": "SOS",
  "mode": "encode"
}

Result

{
  "mode": "encode",
  "result": "... --- ...",
  "skipped": 0,
  "durationSeconds": 1.8
}

Example

{
  "text": ".... ..  /  - .... . .-. ."
}

Result

{
  "mode": "decode",
  "result": "HI THERE",
  "unrecognized": 0
}
text_number_to_wordsNumber to words (English & Arabic)

Spell a number in words in English and Arabic: English cardinal (with decimals, optional British "and"), ordinal (twenty-first), cheque wording for an amount in a currency (cents in words or as 25/100, optional "only"), Arabic cardinal, and Arabic tafqeet for amounts with correct gender/number agreement of the currency noun (optional "faqat … la ghayr" wrapper and the modern 100 spelling). Currencies: JOD, USD, SAR, AED, KWD, EGP, GBP (or none). Accepts Arabic-Indic digits and thousands separators. Up to 999 trillion (15 digits). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/number-words/

InputTypeDescription
number requiredstring | numberThe number or amount, e.g. "1500.250", "1,234,567.89", -12.05 (Arabic-Indic digits work too).
currency"none" | "JOD" | "USD" | "SAR" | "AED" | "KWD" | "EGP" | "GBP"
default "none"		Currency for cheque / tafqeet wording: none (default), JOD, USD, SAR, AED, KWD, EGP, GBP.
onlyboolean
default false		Add "only" (English) and the Arabic "faqat … la ghayr" wrapper to amounts.
britishboolean
default false		British English "and": one hundred and five.
subStyle"words" | "fraction"
default "words"		Cheque sub-units in words (twenty-five cents) or as a fraction (25/100).
modernHundredboolean
default false		Arabic: use the modern spelling of "hundred" (without the extra alif).

Example

{
  "number": "21"
}

Result

{
  "digits": "21",
  "english": "twenty-one",
  "ordinal": "twenty-first",
  "ordinalShort": "21st",
  "arabic": "واحد وعشرون"
}

Example: englishAmount: "One thousand five hundred dollars and twenty-five cents", plus the Arabic tafqeet

{
  "number": "1500.25",
  "currency": "USD"
}

Result

{
  "digits": "1,500.25 USD",
  "english": "one thousand five hundred point two five",
  "arabic": "ألف وخمسمائة فاصلة خمسة وعشرون",
  "englishAmount": "One thousand five hundred dollars and twenty-five cents",
  "arabicAmount": "ألف وخمسمائة دولار وخمسة وعشرون سنتاً"
}
text_readabilityReadability score

Score how easy an English text is to read: Flesch Reading Ease, Flesch-Kincaid Grade, Gunning Fog, SMOG, Coleman-Liau and Automated Readability Index (same formulas, sentence splitter and syllable estimate as the website), plus a verdict, average grade level, and Hemingway-style hints: long sentences (over 20 / 30 words), passive voice and adverbs. The formulas are built for English; for Arabic text the result says so (arabicWarning). Give "text" or a text file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/readability/

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe text to score.
pathstringOr: a path to a UTF-8 text file.

Example: Returns all six scores, the verdict and hints.

{
  "text": "Keep it short. Use plain words. Readers scan, so put the main point first and cut what they don't need."
}

  "advice": "Short sentences and everyday words. Almost anyone can follow it.",
  "averageGrade": 1.7,
  "averageGradeText": "grade 2 · age 7–8",
  "scores": {
    "fleschReadingEase": 111.2,
    "fleschKincaidGrade": {
      "score": -0.6,
      "grade": "kindergarten"
    },
    "gunningFog": {
      "score": 2.7,
      "grade": "grade 3 · age 8–9"
    },
    "smog": {
      "score": 3.1,
      "grade": "grade 3 · age 8–9"
    },
    "colemanLiau": {
      "score": 3,
      "grade": "grade 3 · age 8–9"
    },

      "score": 0.5,
      "grade": "grade 1 · age 6–7"
    }
  },
  "words": 20,
  "sentences": 3,
  "syllablesPerWord": 1.05,
  "wordsPerSentence": 6.7,
  "longSentences": [],
  "passive": [],
  "adverbs": [],

    "No long sentences, passive voice or extra adverbs found."
  ],
  "smogNote": "SMOG is designed for 30+ sentences; with 3 it is a rough estimate."
}
text_slugifySlugify

Turn titles into clean URL slugs, one slug per non-empty line (bulk). Folds accents (é → e, ß → ss), transliterates Arabic to Latin (the article al- becomes its own word) or keeps Arabic letters, converts Arabic-Indic digits, drops tashkeel, turns & into "and", can remove stop words (English and Arabic) and cap the length at a word boundary. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/slugify/

InputTypeDescription
text requiredstringTitle(s) to slugify. Each non-empty line becomes one slug.
separator"-" | "_" | "."
default "-"		Word separator: dash (default), underscore or dot.
lowercaseboolean
default true		Lowercase the slug (default true).
foldAccentsboolean
default true		Transliterate accents: é → e, ß → ss, æ → ae (default true).
removeStopWordsboolean
default false		Remove stop words in English and Arabic such as the, of, and (kept if nothing else is left).
arabic"latin" | "keep"
default "latin"		"latin" transliterates Arabic letters (default); "keep" keeps them.
maxLengthinteger
default 0		Maximum slug length in characters, cut at a word boundary (0 = no limit).

Example

{
  "text": "Crème Brûlée & Café au Lait: A Love Story"
}

Result

{
  "slugs": [
    {
      "title": "Crème Brûlée & Café au Lait: A Love Story",
      "slug": "creme-brulee-and-cafe-au-lait-a-love-story",
      "length": 42,
      "encodedLength": 42
    }
  ]
}

Example: lord-rings

{
  "text": "The Lord of the Rings",
  "removeStopWords": true
}

Result

{
  "slugs": [
    {
      "title": "The Lord of the Rings",
      "slug": "lord-rings",
      "length": 10,
      "encodedLength": 10
    }
  ]
}
text_social_countSocial media character counter

Check a post against platform limits the way the platforms count: X/Twitter weighted length (links = 23, emoji = 2, CJK = 2; limit 280), Threads 500, Bluesky 300, Instagram 2,200 (+ 30 hashtags), LinkedIn 3,000, Facebook 63,206, TikTok 4,000, YouTube title 100 / description 5,000, Pinterest 500, Google title ~60, meta description ~160. Characters are counted as user-perceived graphemes (one emoji = 1). Also: words, hashtags, mentions, links, emoji, and SMS encoding (GSM-7 vs Unicode) with the number of SMS parts and the characters that force Unicode. Give "text" or a text file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/social-counter/

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe post text.
pathstringOr: a path to a UTF-8 text file.
platforms("x" | "threads" | "bsky" | "ig" | "li" | "fb" | "tt" | "yt" | "ytd" | "pin" | "gt" | "gd")[]Only these meters: x, threads, bsky, ig, li, fb, tt, yt, ytd, pin, gt (Google title), gd (meta description). Default: all.
gsmLookalikesboolean
default false		Also return the text with curly quotes, dashes, ellipses, odd spaces etc. replaced by GSM-7 look-alikes (cheaper SMS).

Example: xWeightedLength 48: the link counts 23 and the emoji 2.

{
  "text": "Read it here: https://example.com/blog/code-reviews 🚀 #coding",
  "platforms": [
    "x"
  ]
}

  "lines": 1,

      "id": "x",
      "name": "X (Twitter) post",
      "count": 48,
      "limit": 280,
      "softLimit": false,
      "remaining": 232,
      "over": 0,
      "status": "ok",
      "notes": [
        "Weighted: links 23, emoji 2, CJK 2. Premium allows 25,000."
      ]

    "encoding": "UCS-2",
    "parts": 1,
    "unitsUsed": 62,
    "perPart": 70,
    "leftInPart": 8,
    "nonGsmCharacters": [
      {
        "char": "🚀",
        "count": 1
      }
    ]
  }
}

Works for English and Arabic (tashkeel ignored). Give "text" or a text file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/word-count/

default 12		How many top keywords to return (0 = none).
includeStopWordsboolean
default false		Count common words like "the"/"في" as keywords.

Developer: data formats

JSON, CSV, YAML, XML, SQL, HTML/CSS formatting and conversion, types from JSON, curl to code.

css_formatCSS beautify / minify

Beautify CSS (one declaration per line, one selector per line, blank line between top-level rules, nested @media blocks) or minify it. Strings, url(...) and escapes are kept intact; calc() spacing is preserved. Optional comment removal (/*! … */ kept). Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/html-css-format/

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe CSS as inline text.
pathstringOr: a path to a CSS file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs).
mode"beautify" | "minify"
default "beautify"		"beautify" (default) or "minify".
indent"2" | "4" | "tab" | integer
default "2"		Indentation: 2, 4, "tab" (or any number of spaces 0-8).
removeCommentsboolean
default false		Drop comments (/*! … */ license comments are kept).
outputstringOptional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file.

Example

{
  "text": "a,b{color:red;margin:0 auto}"
}

Result

{
  "result": "a,\nb {\n  color: red;\n  margin: 0 auto;\n}"
}
csv_to_jsonCSV to JSON

Convert CSV/TSV to JSON with an RFC 4180 parser (quoted fields, "" escapes, line breaks inside quotes, CR/LF/CRLF, BOM). Detects the delimiter (comma, semicolon, tab, pipe), uses the first row as headers (blank → column_N, duplicates → name_2), and can type values (numbers, true/false, null; long digit strings like IDs stay strings). Output: array of objects or array of arrays, pretty-printed. Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/csv-json/

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe CSV as inline text.
pathstringOr: a path to a CSV/TSV file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs).
delimiter"auto" | "," | ";" | "tab" | "\t" | "|"
default "auto"		Delimiter: "auto" (detect), ",", ";", "tab" or "|".
headerboolean
default true		First row is a header (default true).
typesboolean
default true		Turn numbers, true/false and empty/null into JSON types (default true).
shape"objects" | "arrays"
default "objects"		"objects" (one object per row) or "arrays" (rows as arrays).
outputstringOptional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file.

Example

{
  "text": "id,zip,ok\n1,007,true"
}

Result

{
  "rows": 1,
  "columns": [
    "id",
    "zip",
    "ok"
  ],
  "delimiter": "comma",
  "ragged": false,
  "result": "[\n  {\n    \"id\": 1,\n    \"zip\": \"007\",\n    \"ok\": true\n  }\n]"
}

…', line continuations, "Copy as cURL" from browsers) into code for JavaScript fetch, axios, Python requests, PHP cURL, Go net/http or Node.js http/https: every target the website supports. Understands -X, -H, -d/--data-raw/--data-binary/--data-urlencode, --json, -F forms and files, -u, -b, -A, -e, -G, -I, -k, -L, -m, --connect-timeout, -x, -T, --compressed, --oauth2-bearer. InputTypeDescription
command requiredstringThe curl command, e.g. "curl -X POST https://api.example.com -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"a":1}'".

revealSecretsboolean
default false		Show Authorization/Cookie/API-key header values and the basic-auth password in the parsed summary (the code always contains them).
outputstringOptional: write the code to this file (single language only).
