}
html_formatHTML beautify / minify

Beautify (indent block elements, keep inline runs on one line) or minify HTML with a forgiving tokenizer: implied end tags (<p>, <li>, <td>…), void elements, and <script>, <style>, <pre>, <textarea> contents kept byte-for-byte. Whitespace between attributes is normalised; attribute values are never changed. Optional comment removal (IE conditional comments kept). Text only: nothing is rendered or run. Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/html-css-format/

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe HTML as inline text.
pathstringOr: a path to a HTML file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs).
mode"beautify" | "minify"
default "beautify"		"beautify" (default) or "minify".
indent"2" | "4" | "tab" | integer
default "2"		Indentation: 2, 4, "tab" (or any number of spaces 0-8).
removeCommentsboolean
default false		Drop comments (IE conditional comments are kept).
outputstringOptional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file.

Example

{
  "text": "<div><p>Hi <b>there</b></p><ul><li>a<li>b</ul></div>"
}

Result

{
  "result": "<div>\n  <p>Hi <b>there</b></p>\n  <ul>\n    <li>a\n    <li>b\n  </ul>\n</div>"
}
json_formatJSON format / minify / validate

Validate, pretty-print (2 or 4 spaces, tab) or minify JSON, optionally sorting object keys (deeply). Invalid JSON gives a clear error with line and column and the lines around it. Also returns size, key count, depth and value count. Give "text" or a file "path"; "output" writes the result to a file. Uses the standard JSON parser (no comments or trailing commas). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/json-studio/

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe JSON as inline text.
pathstringOr: a path to a JSON file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs).
mode"format" | "minify" | "validate"
default "format"		"format" pretty-prints, "minify" removes all whitespace, "validate" only checks (never throws on bad JSON).
indent"2" | "4" | "tab" | integer
default "2"		Indentation: 2, 4, "tab" (or any number of spaces 0-8).
sortKeysboolean
default false		Sort object keys alphabetically at every level.
outputstringOptional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file.

Example

{
  "text": "{\"b\":1,\"a\":[1,2]}",
  "sortKeys": true
}

Result

{
  "valid": true,
  "result": "{\n  \"a\": [\n    1,\n    2\n  ],\n  \"b\": 1\n}"
}

Example: valid: false, line 2, column 1

{
  "text": "{\"a\":1,\n}",
  "mode": "validate"
}

Result

{
  "valid": false,
  "error": "Expected double-quoted property name in JSON at position 8",
  "line": 2,
  "column": 1
}
json_queryJSON query (JSONPath)

Pick values out of JSON with a JSONPath expression. Supported: $ (root, optional), .key, ['key'], [0], [-1], [*], .*, ..key (recursive), [start:end:step] slices, unions [0,2] / ['a','b'], and filters like [?(@.price < 10 && @.tags)] (==, !=, <, <=, >, >=, =~ /regex/i, &&, ||, !, .length). Returns each match with its path (the same path style the website's tree view copies, e.g. $.store.book[0].title). Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/json-studio/

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe JSON as inline text.
pathstringOr: a path to a JSON file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs).
query requiredstringJSONPath expression, e.g. "$.store.book[*].author" or "$..book[?(@.price < 10)].title".
limitinteger
default 1000		Return at most this many matches (default 1000).
outputstringOptional: write the matched values as a JSON array to this file.

Example

{
  "text": "{\"store\":{\"book\":[{\"title\":\"A\",\"price\":8},{\"title\":\"B\",\"price\":12}]}}",
  "query": "$..book[?(@.price < 10)].title"
}

Result

{
  "count": 1,
  "matches": [
    {
      "path": "$.store.book[0].title",
      "value": "A"
    }
  ]
}
json_to_csvJSON to CSV

Convert JSON to CSV. An array of objects becomes one row per object with nested objects/arrays flattened to dotted columns (address.city, tags.0); an array of arrays is written as-is. Fields are quoted only when needed (delimiter, quotes, line breaks, leading/trailing spaces); CRLF line ends. A formula guard (on by default) prefixes cells starting with = + - @ so spreadsheets don't run them. Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/csv-json/

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe JSON (array or object) as inline text.
pathstringOr: a path to a JSON file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs).
delimiter"auto" | "," | ";" | "tab" | "\t" | "|"
default "auto"		Delimiter: "auto" (comma), ",", ";", "tab" or "|".
headerboolean
default true		Include a header row (default true).
safeboolean
default true		Formula guard: prefix cells like =SUM(A1) with ' (default true).
bomboolean
default false		Start with a UTF-8 BOM so Excel reads non-English text correctly.
outputstringOptional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file.

Example

{
  "text": "[{\"a\":{\"b\":1},\"s\":\"say \\\"hi\\\"\"}]"
}

Result

{
  "rows": 1,
  "columns": [
    "a.b",
    "s"
  ],
  "result": "a.b,s\r\n1,\"say \"\"hi\"\"\"\r\n"
}
json_to_typesJSON to types (TypeScript, Go, Python, Zod)

Generate type definitions from a JSON sample: TypeScript interfaces (or type aliases), Go structs with json tags (initialisms like ID/URL, pointers for optional/nullable, optional omitempty), Python dataclasses (snake_case fields with the original key noted) and Zod schemas. All items of an array are merged into one type (missing keys become optional, null makes a field nullable, int+float → number/float64, mixed values → unions); identical shapes share one name. These four are every language the website supports. Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/json-to-types/

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe JSON sample as inline text.
pathstringOr: a path to a JSON file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs).
language"typescript" | "go" | "python" | "zod" | "all"
default "typescript"		Output language, or "all" for all four.
rootNamestring
default "Root"		Name of the top-level type (turned into PascalCase).
exportboolean
default true		TypeScript / Zod: add the export keyword.
tsTypeAliasboolean
default false		TypeScript: use "type X = {…}" instead of "interface X {…}".
goOmitEmptyboolean
default true		Go: add ,omitempty to optional fields.
outputstringOptional: write the code to this file (with language "all", the four outputs joined by headers).

Example

{
  "text": "[{\"a\":1},{\"b\":true}]",
  "rootName": "Root",
  "export": false
}

Result

{
  "types": 1,
  "result": "type Root = RootItem[];\n\ninterface RootItem {\n  a?: number;\n  b?: boolean;\n}\n"
}
json_to_yamlJSON to YAML

Convert JSON to YAML (js-yaml dump: no line wrapping, no anchors; strings that would read as numbers/booleans are quoted). Options: 2 or 4 space indent, sort keys, and write a top-level array as a multi-document YAML stream (--- per item). Invalid JSON gives line and column. Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/yaml-json/

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe JSON as inline text.
pathstringOr: a path to a JSON file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs).
indent2 | 4 | "2" | "4"
default 2		Indentation: 2 or 4 spaces.
sortKeysboolean
default false		Sort mapping keys alphabetically.
multiDocumentboolean
default false		A top-level JSON array becomes one YAML document per item.
outputstringOptional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file.

Example

{
  "text": "{\"b\":1,\"a\":{\"d\":1,\"c\":2}}",
  "sortKeys": true
}

Result

{
  "documents": 1,
  "result": "a:\n  c: 2\n  d: 1\nb: 1\n"
}
markdown_tableMarkdown table

Make a GitHub-flavoured Markdown table from CSV, TSV, a JSON array (of objects or of arrays), an existing Markdown table (to re-align / re-pad it) or a "rows" array. Per-column alignment (left, center, right), columns padded to line up (optional), pipes escaped as \| and line breaks as <br>. CSV delimiter (comma, semicolon, tab) is detected from the first line. Give "text", a file "path" or "rows". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/markdown-table/

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe CSV / TSV / JSON / Markdown table as inline text.
pathstringOr: a path to a CSV/TSV/JSON/Markdown file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs).
rows((string | number | boolean | null)[])[]Or: the table as an array of rows (first row = header unless header is false).
format"auto" | "csv" | "tsv" | "json" | "markdown"
default "auto"		Input format; "auto" detects Markdown, then JSON, then CSV/TSV.
headerboolean
default true		First row is the header. When false, "Column 1", "Column 2"… headers are added.
align"none" | "left" | "center" | "right" | ("none" | "left" | "center" | "right")[]Column alignment: one value for all columns or one per column. Default: keep a Markdown input's alignment, else none.
prettyboolean
default true		Pad cells so the columns line up (default true).
outputstringOptional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file.

Example

{
  "text": "Name,Qty\nApple,3",
  "align": [
    "left",
    "right"
  ]
}

Result

{
  "columns": 2,
  "rows": 1,
  "result": "| Name  | Qty |\n| :---- | --: |\n| Apple |   3 |"
}
markdown_table_parseMarkdown table to JSON / CSV

Read a Markdown table (pipes optional at the ends, \| escapes, <br> as line breaks) back into data: JSON objects keyed by the header, JSON arrays, or CSV; also returns each column's alignment. Uses the website's Markdown import. Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/markdown-table/

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe Markdown table as inline text.
pathstringOr: a path to a Markdown file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs).
as"objects" | "arrays" | "csv"
default "objects"		"objects" (header → keys), "arrays" (header row first) or "csv".
outputstringOptional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file.

Example

{
  "text": "| Name | Qty |\n| :-- | --: |\n| Apple | 3 |"
}

Result

{
  "columns": [
    "Name",
    "Qty"
  ],
  "aligns": [
    "left",
    "right"
  ],
  "rows": 1,
  "data": [
    {
      "Name": "Apple",
      "Qty": "3"
    }
  ]
}
sql_formatSQL format / minify

Format SQL (one clause per line, indented sub-queries, CASE blocks, JOIN … ON with AND/OR, CREATE TABLE column lists) or minify it to one line per statement. Options: keyword case (UPPER, lower, keep), indent (2, 4, tab), trailing or leading commas, keep/drop comments when minifying. Dialect-neutral tokenizer: understands -- and /* */ comments, '' and E'\'' strings, "x", `x` and [x] identifiers, $ dollar quotes, ::casts, JSON operators, :name/$1/? parameters. Only re-spaces; never runs anything. Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/sql-format/

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe SQL as inline text.
pathstringOr: a path to a .sql file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs).
mode"format" | "minify"
default "format"		"format" (default) or "minify".
keywordCase"upper" | "lower" | "preserve"
default "upper"		Keyword and built-in function case.
indent"2" | "4" | "tab" | 2 | 4
default "2"		Indentation: 2, 4 or "tab".
commas"trailing" | "leading"
default "trailing"		Put list commas at line ends (trailing) or starts (leading).
keepCommentsboolean
default true		Minify only: keep comments (as /* */). Optimizer hints /*+ */ and /*! */ are always kept.
outputstringOptional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file.

Example

{
  "text": "select a, b from t where x = 1 and y = 2"
}

Result

{
  "statements": 1,
  "lines": 8,
  "result": "SELECT\n  a,\n  b\nFROM\n  t\nWHERE\n  x = 1\n  AND y = 2"
}
xml_formatXML format / minify / validate / to JSON

Validate XML (well-formedness and namespaces, with line and column for errors), pretty-print it (2, 4 spaces or tab; mixed content, CDATA and xml:space="preserve" kept exactly as written), minify it, or convert it to JSON (attributes as "@name", repeated elements as arrays, text as "#text"). Optionally removes comments. Never loads external DTDs or entities. Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/xml-format/

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe XML as inline text.
pathstringOr: a path to a XML file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs).
mode"format" | "minify" | "json" | "validate"
default "format"		"format" pretty-prints, "minify" strips layout whitespace, "json" converts to JSON, "validate" only checks (never throws on bad XML).
indent"2" | "4" | "tab" | integer
default "2"		Indentation: 2, 4, "tab" (or any number of spaces 0-8).
removeCommentsboolean
default false		Drop <!-- comments --> (format and minify).
outputstringOptional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file.

Example

{
  "text": "<r><a>1</a><b x='1'  y=\"2\"><c/></b></r>"
}

Result

{
  "valid": true,
  "elements": 4,
  "depth": 3,
  "result": "<r>\n  <a>1</a>\n  <b x='1' y=\"2\">\n    <c/>\n  </b>\n</r>"
}

Example

{
  "text": "<a>\n  <b></a>",
  "mode": "validate"
}

Result

{
  "valid": false,
  "error": "Opening and ending tag mismatch: b line 2 and a",
  "line": 2,
  "column": 6
}
xml_queryXML XPath query

Run an XPath 1.0 expression on XML: node-sets (elements, attributes, text…), numbers (count(), sum()), strings and booleans. Full XPath 1.0: all axes, predicates, operators and the core function library. Namespace prefixes declared anywhere in the document can be used as-is; a default namespace on the root gets the prefix "d" (e.g. //d:book), as on the website. Each matched node comes back as XML text plus its type, name and XPath location. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/xml-format/

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe XML as inline text.
pathstringOr: a path to a XML file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs).
xpath requiredstringXPath 1.0 expression, e.g. "//book[price > 30]/title", "count(//item)", "//d:entry/@id".
namespacesobjectExtra or overriding prefix → namespace URI bindings, e.g. {"atom":"http://www.w3.org/2005/Atom"}.
limitinteger
default 1000		Return at most this many nodes (default 1000).

Example

{
  "text": "<lib><book><price>42</price><title>A</title></book><book><price>9</price><title>B</title></book></lib>",
  "xpath": "//book[price > 30]/title"
}

Result

{
  "kind": "nodes",
  "count": 1,
  "nodes": [
    {
      "type": "element",
      "name": "title",
      "location": "/lib/book[1]/title",
      "text": "<title>A</title>"
    }
  ]
}
yaml_to_jsonYAML to JSON

Convert YAML to JSON (YAML 1.2 core schema plus anchors/aliases, << merge keys, timestamps, multi-document files). Safe: no code tags (!!js/function, !!python… are rejected), alias bombs and recursive aliases are stopped. Several documents become a JSON array. Errors give line and column. Give "text" or a file "path". Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/yaml-json/

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe YAML as inline text.
pathstringOr: a path to a YAML file (UTF-8; use this for big inputs).
indent2 | 4 | "2" | "4"
default 2		Indentation: 2 or 4 spaces.
sortKeysboolean
default false		Sort object keys alphabetically at every level.
alwaysArrayboolean
default false		Always output an array of documents, even for one document.
outputstringOptional: also write the result to this file path (or folder ending in "/"). The result text is then left out of the reply. Results over 1,000,000 characters are always written to a file.

Example

{
  "text": "base: &b {x: 1}\nchild:\n  <<: *b\n  y: 2\n"
}

Result

{
  "documents": 1,
  "values": 6,
  "result": "{\n  \"base\": {\n    \"x\": 1\n  },\n  \"child\": {\n    \"x\": 1,\n    \"y\": 2\n  }\n}\n"
}

Developer: utilities

Hashes, Base64, URLs, JWT, IDs, regex, cron, timestamps, subnets, chmod, HTTP status, .gitignore.

base_convertNumber base convert

Convert a whole number of any size (BigInt) between bases 2–36: binary, octal, decimal, hex, base 36 or any other. Input base can be auto-detected from 0x / 0b / 0o prefixes; spaces, _ and , separators are ignored; negatives OK. Also shows bit length and the two's complement view (signed/unsigned value, hex, bits) at 8/16/32/64 bits, and whether it fits. Fractions are not supported (same as the website). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/base-convert/

InputTypeDescription
value requiredstringThe number, e.g. "0xC0FFEE", "-0b101", "1_000_000", "zz" (with from=36).
from"auto" | integer
default "auto"		Input base 2–36, or "auto" (prefix, else decimal).
tointeger[]
default [2,8,10,16,36]		Output bases. Default [2, 8, 10, 16, 36].
groupboolean
default false		Group digits like the website (binary/hex by 4, octal by 3, decimal with commas).
uppercaseboolean
default false		Upper-case letters for bases above 10.
width8 | 16 | 32 | 64
default 32		Bit width for the two's complement view.

Example: decimal 12648430, binary 110000001111111111101110

{
  "value": "0xC0FFEE"
}

Result

  "decimal": "12648430",
  "results": [
    {

      "value": "110000001111111111101110"
    },
    {
      "base": 8,
      "name": "Octal",
      "value": "60177756"
    },
    {
      "base": 10,
      "name": "Decimal",
      "value": "12648430"
    },
    {
      "base": 16,
      "name": "Hex",
      "value": "c0ffee"
    },
    {
      "base": 36,
      "name": "Base 36",
      "value": "7j3la"
    }
  ],
  "bitLength": 24,
  "bytes": 3,
  "twosComplement": {
    "width": 32,
    "signed": "12648430",
    "unsigned": "12648430",
    "hex": "0x00C0 FFEE",
    "binary": "00000000110000001111111111101110",
    "fits": true,
    "note": "Fits in 32 bits. Signed range -2147483648 to 2147483647."
  }
}

{
  "value": "-1",
  "width": 8
}

base64_decodeBase64 decode

Decode Base64 (standard or URL-safe, padding optional, whitespace and data: URI prefix allowed). Valid UTF-8 text is returned inline; binary data (or anything when "output" is given) is written to a file whose extension comes from the detected type (PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, BMP, PDF, ZIP, gzip, MP3, Ogg, MP4, WOFF2, SVG, JSON). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/base64-kit/

InputTypeDescription
base64 requiredstringThe Base64 text to decode (may be a data: URI).
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
fileNamestringBase name for the written file (extension follows the detected type). Default "decoded".

Example

{
  "base64": "Zm9vYmFy"
}

Result

{
  "text": "foobar",
  "bytes": 6,
  "isText": true,
  "detectedType": "Text",
  "mimeType": "text/plain"
}

Example: Writes decoded.png

{
  "base64": "data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAAEAAAABCAYAAAAfFcSJAAAADUlEQVR42mNkYPhfDwAChwGA60e6kgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg=="
}

Result

{
  "bytes": 70,
  "isText": false,
  "detectedType": "PNG image",
  "mimeType": "image/png",
  "dataUriMime": "image/png",
  "path": "C:\\Users\\User\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\s3eidat-docs-2G2NgY\\decoded.png"
}
base64_encodeBase64 encode

Encode text (as UTF-8) or a file (path or base64) to Base64. Options: URL-safe alphabet (- and _, no padding), wrap at 76 columns (MIME), or a data: URI for files (MIME type detected from magic bytes). Results over 1 MB, or when "output" is given, are written to a text file instead of returned inline. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/base64-kit/

InputTypeDescription
textstringText to encode (UTF-8).
filestringOr: a file to encode: local path or base64 data.
urlSafeboolean
default false		Use the URL-safe alphabet (- and _) and drop "=" padding.
wrapboolean
default false		Insert a line break every 76 characters (MIME style).
dataUriboolean
default false		Return a data: URI (data:<mime>;base64,…) instead of bare Base64.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).

Example

{
  "text": "foobar"
}

Result

{
  "base64": "Zm9vYmFy",
  "inputBytes": 6,
  "characters": 8
}

Example: URL-safe output without padding

{
  "text": "ûÿ",
  "urlSafe": true
}

Result

{
  "inputBytes": 4,
  "characters": 6,
  "base64": "w7vDvw"
}

Turn text into its UTF-8 bytes (hex, binary or decimal), or bytes back into text. Byte input is lenient: "48 65", "4865", "0x48,0x69", "01001000 01101001", "72 105". Invalid UTF-8 is reported and shown with � replacements. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/base-convert/

  "characters": 4,
  "validUtf8": true
}
chmod_calcchmod calculator

Convert Unix file permissions between octal (755, 0644, 4755), symbolic (rwxr-xr-x, ls -l style -rwsr-xr-x) and chmod clauses (u=rwx,g=rx,o=; relative ones like g+w or +x apply to "base"). Returns all forms, a plain-English description per owner/group/others, setuid/setgid/sticky meaning, safety warnings, and ready chmod commands. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/chmod-calc/

cron_explainCron explain

Explain a standard 5-field cron expression (minute hour day-of-month month day-of-week; names like MON/JAN, ranges, lists, steps, ? and the @hourly/@daily/@weekly/@monthly/@yearly/@reboot macros) in plain English and list the next run times in an IANA timezone. Follows Vixie cron: when both day fields are restricted a day matches either one. Quartz 6/7-field and L/W/# syntax are rejected with a clear message; per-field errors are reported. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/cron-explain/

InputTypeDescription
expression requiredstringThe cron expression, e.g. "*/30 9-17 * * 1-5" or "@daily".
countinteger
default 10		How many upcoming run times to list (0–100). Default 10.
timezonestringIANA timezone the cron daemon runs in. Default: the server's (Asia/Amman).
fromstringStart searching after this instant (ISO 8601). Default: now.
hour12boolean
default false		Describe times on a 12-hour clock (9:00 AM) instead of 24-hour (09:00).

Example: description: "At 09:00, on Monday through Friday"

{
  "expression": "0 9 * * 1-5",
  "count": 3,
  "timezone": "Europe/London"
}

css_clampFluid CSS clamp()

Build a fluid typography/spacing value: CSS clamp() that grows linearly from a size at a small viewport to a size at a large viewport (sizes and viewports in px), expressed in rem or px. Also returns the computed size at sample viewport widths. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/css-units/

InputTypeDescription
minSizenumber
default 16		Size in px at the small viewport. Default 16.
maxSizenumber
default 32		Size in px at the large viewport. Default 32.
minViewportnumber
default 320		Small viewport width in px. Default 320.
maxViewportnumber
default 1280		Large viewport width in px. Default 1280.
unit"rem" | "px"
default "rem"		Output unit. Default rem.
rootFontSizenumber
default 16		Root (html) font size in px, for rem. Default 16.
sampleViewportsnumber[]
default [320,768,1280,1920]		Viewport widths (px) to compute the resulting size at.

Example

{
  "minSize": 16,
  "maxSize": 32,
  "minViewport": 320,
  "maxViewport": 1280
}

Result

{
  "css": "clamp(1rem, 0.6667rem + 1.6667vw, 2rem)"
}
css_unitsCSS unit converter

Convert a CSS length between px, rem, em, %, pt, pc, in, cm, mm, vw, vh, vmin and vmax, using a root font size, parent font size and viewport size (defaults 16px / 16px / 1440×900). Uses the CSS definition of 96px per inch. Returns every unit, rounded to 4 decimals by default. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/css-units/

InputTypeDescription
value requirednumberThe length to convert, e.g. 24.

default "px"		Unit of "value". Default px.
rootFontSizenumber
default 16		Root (html) font size in px, for rem. Default 16.
parentFontSizenumber
default 16		Parent element font size in px, for em and %. Default 16.
viewportWidthnumber
default 1440		Viewport width in px, for vw/vmin/vmax. Default 1440.
viewportHeightnumber
default 900		Viewport height in px, for vh/vmin/vmax. Default 900.
decimalsinteger
default 4		Decimal places to round to. Default 4.

gitignore_generateGenerate .gitignore

Build a .gitignore by combining the website's templates for languages, frameworks, mobile, game engines, tools, OSes and editors (node, python, java, android, ios, flutter, reactnative, unity, unreal, go, rust, cpp, dotnet, php, wordpress, rails, django, nextjs, vite, docker, terraform, macos, windows, linux, vscode, jetbrains, vim). Templates can be named by id, name or keyword (e.g. "javascript" → node). Duplicate patterns are removed, optional "### Name ###" section headers, plus your own custom lines at the end. Returns the text, or writes it to "output" (e.g. "./.gitignore"). Set "list" to see all templates. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/gitignore-gen/

InputTypeDescription
templatesstring[]
default []		Templates to combine, in order, e.g. ["node", "nextjs", "vscode", "macos"].
customstringExtra lines to append in a "Custom" section.
headersboolean
default true		Add a generated-by header, section headers and template comments. Default true.
dedupeboolean
default true		Drop patterns that already appeared in an earlier section. Default true.
listboolean
default false		Just list the available templates (id, name, group, keywords).
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).

Example

{
  "templates": [
    "node",
    "nextjs",
    "vscode",
    "macos"
  ],
  "custom": "secrets/"
}

}

hash_computeHash (MD5 / SHA)

Compute MD5, SHA-1, SHA-256 and SHA-512 of text or a file (path or base64), optionally an HMAC with a key, and check against an expected checksum (hex in any case/spacing, "SHA256 (file) = …" lines, or base64). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/hash-lab/

InputTypeDescription
textstringText to hash (UTF-8).
filestringOr: a file to hash: local path or base64.
hmacKeystringIf set, also compute HMAC with this key.
hmacAlgorithm"SHA-1" | "SHA-256" | "SHA-384" | "SHA-512"
default "SHA-256"
format"hex" | "HEX" | "base64"
default "hex"		Output encoding.
expectedstringA checksum to compare against; the result says which algorithm matched.

Example: SHA-256 = ba7816bf8f01cfea414140de5dae2223b00361a396177a9cb410ff61f20015ad

{
  "text": "abc"
}

Result

{
  "bytes": 3,
  "hashes": {
    "MD5": "900150983cd24fb0d6963f7d28e17f72",
    "SHA-1": "a9993e364706816aba3e25717850c26c9cd0d89d",
    "SHA-256": "ba7816bf8f01cfea414140de5dae2223b00361a396177a9cb410ff61f20015ad",
    "SHA-512": "ddaf35a193617abacc417349ae20413112e6fa4e89a97ea20a9eeee64b55d39a2192992a274fc1a836ba3c23a3feebbd454d4423643ce80e2a9ac94fa54ca49f"
  }
}
http_statusHTTP status codes

Look up HTTP status codes: by exact code (e.g. 418, also non-standard ones like 499 or 524), by code prefix ("50"), or by words ("too many", "redirect post"), optionally limited to a class (1xx–5xx). Each result has the name, what it means, when to use it, common causes and the defining spec. Non-standard nginx/Cloudflare codes are only searched when "includeNonStandard" is set. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/http-status/

InputTypeDescription
querystring
default ""		A code ("404"), code prefix ("5", "50") or search words. Empty = all codes (filtered by class).
class"1" | "2" | "3" | "4" | "5"Only this class: 1 informational, 2 success, 3 redirection, 4 client error, 5 server error.
includeNonStandardboolean
default false		Also search non-standard codes (nginx 499, Cloudflare 52x…).
limitinteger
default 20		Max results to return. Default 20.

Example: 429 Too Many Requests with meaning, when to use and causes

{
  "query": "429"
}

Result

{
  "count": 1,
  "results": [
    {
      "code": 429,
      "name": "Too Many Requests",
      "class": "4xx",
      "className": "Client error",
      "meaning": "You sent too many requests in a given time (rate limiting).",
      "whenToUse": "Rate limits. Send Retry-After to say when to try again.",
      "commonCauses": "Scripts and bots looping fast, missing back-off, shared IPs.",
      "spec": "RFC 6585",
      "nonStandard": false
    }
  ]
}

Example

{
  "query": "teapot"
}

Result

{
  "count": 1,
  "results": [
    {
      "code": 418,
      "name": "I'm a teapot",
      "class": "4xx",
      "className": "Client error",
      "meaning": "An April Fools' joke: the server refuses to brew coffee because it is a teapot.",
      "whenToUse": "Easter eggs. Some sites use it to answer requests they want to refuse.",
      "commonCauses": "Hyper Text Coffee Pot Control Protocol jokes, bot blocking.",
      "spec": "RFC 2324",
      "nonStandard": false
    }
  ]
}
id_generateGenerate IDs (UUID / ULID / NanoID)

Generate 1–1000 unique IDs with a cryptographic RNG: UUID v4 (random), UUID v7 (time-ordered, RFC 9562, strictly increasing within a batch), ULID (26 Crockford Base32 chars, monotonic) or NanoID (custom length 2–128 and alphabet: URL-safe default, alphanumeric, hex, digits, no look-alikes, or your own). Options: uppercase, no dashes. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/uuid-forge/

InputTypeDescription
kind"v4" | "v7" | "ulid" | "nanoid"
default "v4"		Which ID type.
countinteger
default 1		How many IDs (1–1000).
uppercaseboolean
default false		Upper-case output (UUID hex digits, ULID letters). Default false = lower case, like the website.
noDashesboolean
default false		UUIDs only: drop the dashes (32 hex chars).
lengthinteger
default 21		NanoID length (2–128). 21 URL-safe chars ≈ UUID v4 collision safety.
alphabetstring
default "A-Za-z0-9_-"		NanoID alphabet: a preset ("A-Za-z0-9_-", "A-Za-z0-9", "0-9a-f", "0-9", "nolook") or your own characters (2–256 distinct; duplicates ignored).

Example: Three time-ordered UUIDs, e.g. 019a2f3c-8d41-7a0b-9c2e-5f1d3b7a4c10

{
  "kind": "v7",
  "count": 3
}

Example

{
  "kind": "nanoid",
  "length": 12,
  "alphabet": "nolook"
}
id_inspectInspect a UUID / ULID

Check whether a string is a valid UUID (any version; braces, urn:uuid: and missing dashes accepted) or ULID, and report its version, variant and canonical form. For UUID v1, v6, v7 and ULID also returns the embedded creation time. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/uuid-forge/

InputTypeDescription
id requiredstringThe UUID or ULID to inspect.

Example

  "id": "017F22E2-79B0-7CC3-98C4-DC0C0C07398F"
}

  "canonical": "017f22e2-79b0-7cc3-98c4-dc0c0c07398f",
  "createdAt": "2022-02-22T19:22:22.000Z",
  "timestampMs": 1645557742000
}
ip_in_rangeIs this IP in that range?

Check whether an IPv4 or IPv6 address falls inside a range given as CIDR ("10.0.0.0/8", "2001:db8::/32"), a dash range ("192.168.1.10 - 192.168.1.60") or a single address. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/subnet-calc/

InputTypeDescription
ip requiredstringThe address to test.
range requiredstringCIDR, "start - end" range, or single address (same family as "ip").

Example

{
  "ip": "192.168.10.77",
  "range": "192.168.10.64/27"
}

Result

{
  "inside": true,
  "rangeStart": "192.168.10.64",
  "rangeEnd": "192.168.10.95"
}
jwt_decodeJWT decode

Decode a JSON Web Token (a leading "Bearer " is fine): header, payload, iat/nbf/exp/auth_time as ISO dates with relative time, expired / not-yet-valid status, and named standard claims. If "secret" is given, verifies HS256/HS384/HS512 signatures (secret as text or Base64URL). RS/ES/PS tokens are decoded but not verified (needs a public key). Encrypted JWE tokens can't be read. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/jwt-inspector/

InputTypeDescription
token requiredstringThe JWT (header.payload.signature).
secretstringHMAC secret to verify an HS256/384/512 signature.
secretIsBase64boolean
default false		The secret is Base64/Base64URL-encoded bytes, not text.
nownumberUnix time in seconds to judge expiry against. Default: now.

Example: payload.name = "John Doe", signature.verified = true, status "No expiry (exp) claim"

{
  "token": "eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiIsInR5cCI6IkpXVCJ9.eyJzdWIiOiIxMjM0NTY3ODkwIiwibmFtZSI6IkpvaG4gRG9lIiwiaWF0IjoxNTE2MjM5MDIyfQ.SflKxwRJSMeKKF2QT4fwpMeJf36POk6yJV_adQssw5c",
  "secret": "your-256-bit-secret"
}

Result

{
  "header": {
    "alg": "HS256",
    "typ": "JWT"
  },
  "payload": {
    "sub": "1234567890",
    "name": "John Doe",
    "iat": 1516239022
  },
  "algorithm": "HS256",
  "unsigned": false,
  "status": "warn",
  "statusText": "No expiry (exp) claim",
  "expired": false,
  "times": [
    {
      "claim": "iat",
      "label": "Issued at",
      "value": 1516239022,
      "iso": "2018-01-18T01:30:22.000Z",
      "relative": "8 years ago"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "claim": "sub",
      "label": "Subject",
      "value": "1234567890"
    },
    {
      "claim": "name",
      "label": "Name",
      "value": "John Doe"
    }
  ],
  "signature": {
    "verified": true,
    "message": "Signature verified"
  }
}
meta_tags_generateMeta tags generator

Generate the <head> HTML for SEO and social sharing: <title>, meta description, canonical link, Open Graph (og:type, url, title, description, image + alt, site_name, locale) and X/Twitter card tags. All values are HTML-escaped. Also checks lengths (title 30–60, description 70–160 characters), URLs and the @handle, and shows how Google, Facebook and X would truncate the title and description. URLs without a scheme get https://. Optionally writes the tags to an .html file. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/meta-tags/

siteNamestring
default ""		Site name (og:site_name).
twitterstring
default ""		X/Twitter handle: "@name", "name" or a profile URL.
typestring
default "website"		og:type, e.g. website, article, product, profile. Default website.
card"summary_large_image" | "summary"
default "summary_large_image"		X card type. Default summary_large_image.
localestring
default ""		og:locale, e.g. en_US or ar_AR.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).

, $). Flags g i m s u y (and d, v). Runs in an isolated worker with a timeout (default 1.5 s) so catastrophic backtracking is reported instead of hanging. Lists up to 5000 matches. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/regex-playground/

InputTypeDescription
pattern requiredstringThe regex source, without slashes, e.g. "(\\d+)-(?<w>[a-z]+)".
flagsstring
default "g"		Regex flags, default "g".
text requiredstringThe text to search.
replacestringReplacement string for a replace preview (JavaScript String.replace syntax).
limitinteger
default 500		Max matches to include in the result (count is still total). Default 500.
timeoutMsinteger
default 1500		Kill the regex after this many ms. Default 1500.

Example: 2 matches (index 0 and 5), replaced = "ab:1 cd:22 x"

{
  "pattern": "(\\d+)-(?<w>[a-z]+)",
  "flags": "g",
  "text": "1-ab 22-cd x",
  "replace": "{{body}}lt;w>:$1"
}

Result

{
  "count": 2,
  "more": false,
  "groupNames": [
    null,
    "w"
  ],
  "matches": [
    {
      "index": 0,
      "length": 4,
      "text": "1-ab",
      "groups": [
        "1",
        "ab"
      ],
      "named": {
        "w": "ab"
      }
    },
    {
      "index": 5,
      "length": 5,
      "text": "22-cd",
      "groups": [
        "22",
        "cd"
      ],
      "named": {
        "w": "cd"
      }
    }
  ],
  "replaced": "ab:1 cd:22 x"
}
subnet_calcSubnet calculator (IPv4 / IPv6)

IPv4: from "192.168.1.0/26", "10.0.0.5 255.255.255.0" or a wildcard mask, get network, broadcast, first/last host, usable and total addresses, netmask, wildcard, class, address type (private, CGNAT, loopback, documentation…), integer/hex/binary forms; optionally split into N equal subnets or allocate VLSM blocks by host counts ("Sales:50, Ops:20"). /31 and /32 follow RFC 3021. IPv6: compressed (RFC 5952) and expanded forms, network, first/last address, address count, number of /64s, reverse DNS name and type. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/subnet-calc/

InputTypeDescription
address requiredstringIPv4 or IPv6 address, optionally with /prefix (or an IPv4 dotted mask after a space or slash).
prefixintegerPrefix length to use when "address" has none (default /24 for IPv4, /128 for IPv6), or to override it.
splitintegerIPv4: split the network into at least this many equal subnets (rounded up to a power of two; first 4096 listed).
vlsmstringIPv4: VLSM host requirements, e.g. "50, 20, 10" or "Sales:50, Ops:20". Largest first, each block aligned.

Example: broadcast 192.168.1.63, hosts 192.168.1.1–192.168.1.62, 62 usable, mask 255.255.255.192

{
  "address": "192.168.1.0/26"
}

Result

{
  "version": 4,
  "address": "192.168.1.0",
  "prefix": 26,
  "network": "192.168.1.0/26",
  "broadcast": "192.168.1.63",
  "firstHost": "192.168.1.1",
  "lastHost": "192.168.1.62",
  "usableHosts": 62,
  "totalAddresses": 64,
  "subnetMask": "255.255.255.192",
  "wildcardMask": "0.0.0.63",
  "class": "C",
  "type": "Private (RFC 1918)",
  "isPublic": false,

}

time_convertTimestamp convert

Convert a Unix timestamp (seconds, ms, µs or ns — auto-detected from the number of digits, or set "unit") or a date string to ISO 8601 (UTC and in a timezone), RFC 7231 / UTC string, local time in any IANA timezone with its offset, relative time ("3 hours ago"), day of year, ISO week, and Unix seconds/ms. A wall-clock time without an offset (e.g. "2026-10-26T14:30") is read in "timezone". Also shows the same instant in extra "zones" (world clock). No value = now. Date arithmetic is not done here. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/timestamp-convert/

InputTypeDescription
valuestring | numberA Unix timestamp (e.g. 1700000000 or "1700000000123"), an ISO/RFC date string, or a wall-clock "YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM[:SS]". Default: now.
unit"auto" | "s" | "ms" | "us" | "ns"
default "auto"		Unit of a numeric timestamp. Default: auto-detect.
timezonestringIANA timezone for the local view and for wall-clock input. Default: the server's (Asia/Amman).
zonesstring[]Extra IANA timezones to show the same instant in, e.g. ["UTC","Asia/Tokyo"].
nownumberReference "now" in Unix ms for the relative time. Default: the current time.

Example: iso 2023-11-14T22:13:20.000Z, localIso 2023-11-15T01:13:20+03:00, detectedUnit seconds

{
  "value": 1700000000,
  "timezone": "Asia/Amman"
}

Result

{
  "input": "1700000000",
  "unixSeconds": 1700000000,
  "unixMilliseconds": 1700000000000,
  "iso": "2023-11-14T22:13:20.000Z",
  "utc": "Tue, 14 Nov 2023 22:13:20 GMT",
  "timezone": "Asia/Amman",
  "local": "Wed, Nov 15, 2023, 01:13:20 AM",
  "localIso": "2023-11-15T01:13:20+03:00",
  "offset": "UTC+03:00",
  "relative": "2 years ago",
  "dayOfYear": 319,
  "isoWeek": 46,
  "detectedUnit": "seconds"
}

Example: unixSeconds 1782864000 (2026-07-01T00:00:00Z)

{
  "value": "2026-07-01T09:00",
  "timezone": "Asia/Tokyo"
}

Result

{
  "input": "2026-07-01T09:00",
  "unixSeconds": 1782864000,
  "unixMilliseconds": 1782864000000,
  "iso": "2026-07-01T00:00:00.000Z",
  "utc": "Wed, 01 Jul 2026 00:00:00 GMT",
  "timezone": "Asia/Tokyo",
  "local": "Wed, Jul 1, 2026, 09:00:00 AM",
  "localIso": "2026-07-01T09:00:00+09:00",
  "offset": "UTC+09:00",
  "relative": "in 5 months",
  "dayOfYear": 182,
  "isoWeek": 27
}
url_decodeURL decode

Decode percent-encoded text (decodeURIComponent, or decodeURI with mode "full"). Optionally turn "+" into spaces (form encoding). Errors on malformed %-sequences. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/url-toolkit/

InputTypeDescription
text requiredstringPercent-encoded text.
mode"component" | "full"
default "component"		"component" = encodeURIComponent (for one query value or path segment; escapes / ? & = #). "full" = encodeURI (a whole URL; keeps those).
plusAsSpaceboolean
default false		Treat "+" as a space (application/x-www-form-urlencoded).

Example

{
  "text": "x+y%20%E2%9C%93",
  "plusAsSpace": true
}

Result

{
  "result": "x y ✓"
}
url_encodeURL encode

Percent-encode text for URLs, exactly like JavaScript encodeURIComponent ("component", default) or encodeURI ("full"). UTF-8 based. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/url-toolkit/

InputTypeDescription
text requiredstringText to encode.
mode"component" | "full"
default "component"		"component" = encodeURIComponent (for one query value or path segment; escapes / ? & = #). "full" = encodeURI (a whole URL; keeps those).

Example

{
  "text": "a b&c/d?é"
}

Result

{
  "result": "a%20b%26c%2Fd%3F%C3%A9"
}
url_parseURL parse

Split a URL into protocol, user, host, port, path, query, fragment and origin (with decoded forms), list its query parameters in order (duplicates and bare flags kept, + as space) and flag tracking parameters (utm_*, fbclid, gclid, msclkid, mc_cid, _ga, _hsenc…). Optionally returns the URL with tracking removed. A missing scheme means https://. Passwords are never echoed. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/url-toolkit/

InputTypeDescription
url requiredstringThe URL to parse.
stripTrackingboolean
default false		Also return "cleanUrl" with tracking parameters removed.

Example: cleanUrl = https://example.com/shop/item?id=42#reviews, removed = 2

{
  "url": "https://example.com/shop/item?id=42&utm_source=newsletter&fbclid=IwAR0xYz#reviews",
  "stripTracking": true
}

Result

{
  "parts": {
    "href": "https://example.com/shop/item?id=42&utm_source=newsletter&fbclid=IwAR0xYz#reviews",
    "protocol": "https",
    "username": "",
    "hasPassword": false,
    "host": "example.com",
    "port": "",
    "defaultPort": "443",
    "path": "/shop/item",
    "query": "id=42&utm_source=newsletter&fbclid=IwAR0xYz",
    "fragment": "reviews",
    "origin": "https://example.com"
  },
  "params": [
    {
      "key": "id",
      "value": "42",
      "tracking": false
    },
    {
      "key": "utm_source",
      "value": "newsletter",
      "tracking": true
    },
    {
      "key": "fbclid",
      "value": "IwAR0xYz",
      "tracking": true
    }
  ],
  "trackingCount": 2,
  "cleanUrl": "https://example.com/shop/item?id=42#reviews",
  "removed": 2
}
useragent_parseUser agent parse

Parse browser user-agent strings into browser + version, engine (Blink, WebKit, Gecko, Trident, EdgeHTML, Presto), OS + version, device type (desktop, mobile, tablet, TV, console, bot, HTTP client), vendor and model. Recognises ~50 crawlers and AI bots (Googlebot, Bingbot, GPTBot, ClaudeBot, PerplexityBot…) and HTTP clients (curl, python-requests, Go, okhttp, Postman…). Give one "userAgent", a list, or a text/log file (one per line, or the last quoted field of each access-log line); up to 5000 per call, with a count by type. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/ua-parser/

InputTypeDescription
userAgentstringOne user-agent string.
userAgentsstring[]Several user-agent strings.
filestringOr: a text file (path or base64) with one user agent per line, or an access log.
accessLogboolean
default false		Read "file" as an access log: take the last quoted field of each line.

Example: Mobile Safari 17.4, WebKit, iOS 17.4, mobile, Apple iPhone

{
  "userAgent": "Mozilla/5.0 (iPhone; CPU iPhone OS 17_4 like Mac OS X) AppleWebKit/605.1.15 (KHTML, like Gecko) Version/17.4 Mobile/15E148 Safari/604.1"
}

Result

{
  "count": 1,
  "byType": {
    "mobile": 1
  },
  "results": [
    {
      "ua": "Mozilla/5.0 (iPhone; CPU iPhone OS 17_4 like Mac OS X) AppleWebKit/605.1.15 (KHTML, like Gecko) Version/17.4 Mobile/15E148 Safari/604.1",
      "browser": "Mobile Safari",
      "browserVersion": "17.4",
      "engine": "WebKit",
      "engineVersion": "605.1.15",
      "os": "iOS",
      "osVersion": "17.4",
      "type": "mobile",
      "vendor": "Apple",
      "model": "iPhone",
      "bot": "",
      "client": "",
      "typeLabel": "Mobile",
      "summary": {
        "browser": "Mobile Safari 17.4",
        "engine": "WebKit 605.1.15",
        "os": "iOS 17.4",
        "device": "Apple iPhone"
      }
    }
  ]
}

Calculators

Loans, interest, percentages, salary, GPA, zakat, BMI, dates, units, Hijri, maths.

calc_ageAge calculator

Exact age in years, months and days from a date of birth (on today or any "on" date), with totals in months, weeks, days and hours, the weekday of birth, Hijri (Umm al-Qura) birth date and Hijri age, next birthday (Feb 29 birthdays fall on Feb 28 in common years), star sign, Chinese zodiac, the next 1,000-day milestone, and optionally the age gap to a second person. Dates are ISO YYYY-MM-DD. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/age-calc/

InputTypeDescription
birthDate requiredstringDate of birth, YYYY-MM-DD.
onDatestringWork out the age on this date (YYYY-MM-DD). Default: today.
otherBirthDatestringOptional second person's date of birth, to compare.
otherNamestringName of the second person (default "They").

Example: 32 years, 5 months, 11 days

{
  "birthDate": "1994-05-15",
  "onDate": "2026-10-26"
}

}
calc_bmiBMI calculator

Body mass index with the WHO adult category, the healthy weight range for your height (BMI 18.5–24.9), BMI Prime, optional waist-to-height ratio, and for adults (18–120, with age) BMR by Mifflin-St Jeor plus daily calories to maintain or lose ~0.5 kg (1 lb) a week at five activity levels. Metric (cm, kg) or imperial (ft + in, lb). Height 50–272 cm, weight 10–650 kg. Not medical advice; children need percentile charts. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/bmi-calc/

InputTypeDescription
units"metric" | "imperial"
default "metric"		metric: cm and kg; imperial: ft + in and lb.
heightCmnumber | stringHeight in cm (metric).
heightFtnumber | stringHeight, feet part (imperial).
heightInnumber | stringHeight, inches part (imperial).
weightnumber | stringWeight in kg (metric) or lb (imperial).
agenumber | stringAge in years (adults 18+ get BMR and calories).
sex"m" | "f"
default "m"		Sex, for BMR (m or f).
waistnumber | stringOptional waist size in cm (metric) or inches (imperial).

Example: BMI 22.9 Healthy weight, BMR 1649 kcal

{
  "heightCm": 175,
  "weight": 70,
  "age": 30,
  "sex": "m"
}

calc_cash_countCash counter

Count a cash drawer by denomination with exact integer maths (no float errors): JOD (3 decimals, fils), USD, EUR, SAR, AED, GBP, KWD, EGP, or a custom currency (your own note and coin values, 0/2/3 decimals). Give how many of each note/coin (by value like 50 or 0.25, by label like "50 JD" or "¼ dinar"); returns per-denomination amounts, the total, notes and coins counted, and over/short against an expected amount. list=true shows the denominations of a currency. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/cash-counter/

InputTypeDescription
currency"JOD" | "USD" | "EUR" | "SAR" | "AED" | "GBP" | "KWD" | "EGP" | "CUSTOM"
default "JOD"		Currency preset, or CUSTOM.
customobjectCUSTOM currency definition.
countsobject[]
default []		What you counted.
expectednumber | stringExpected total, to show over/short.
notestringOptional note for the summary (e.g. "Evening shift").
listboolean
default false		Just list the denominations.

Example: total 151.350 JD, short by 0.050

{
  "currency": "JOD",
  "counts": [
    {
      "denomination": 50,
      "count": 3
    },
    {
      "denomination": 0.5,
      "count": 2
    },
    {
      "denomination": 0.05,
      "count": 7
    }
  ],
  "expected": "151.400"
}

calc_compound_interestCompound interest / savings growth

Project savings with compound interest: an initial deposit plus monthly contributions (at the start or end of each month), a nominal annual rate compounded yearly, half-yearly, quarterly, monthly, daily or continuously, optionally adjusted for inflation ("today's money"). Returns final balance, total deposits, interest earned and a year-by-year table. Rate -50–100 %, 1 month to 100 years. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/compound-interest/

InputTypeDescription
initialnumber | stringInitial deposit (default 0).
monthlynumber | stringMonthly contribution (default 0).
annualRate requirednumber | stringNominal annual interest rate in percent, e.g. 7.
years requirednumber | stringDuration in years (fractions allowed, e.g. 1.5; at least one month).
compounding"yearly" | "half-yearly" | "quarterly" | "monthly" | "daily" | "continuously"
default "monthly"		How often interest compounds.
timing"end" | "start"
default "end"		Contributions at the end (ordinary annuity) or start (annuity due) of each month.
inflationnumber | stringOptional yearly inflation in percent, to show the balance in today's money.
tableboolean
default true		Include the year-by-year table.

calc_date_diffDate difference / add days

Two date jobs. difference: days between two dates (optionally counting the end day), as years/months/days, weeks, whole months, working days and weekend days for a chosen weekend (Fri–Sat default, Sat–Sun, Fri only, Sun only; public holidays are not counted). add: add or subtract days, business days, weeks, months or years to a date (month ends clamp: Jan 31 + 1 month = Feb 28/29). Results include the weekday and Umm al-Qura Hijri date. Dates are ISO YYYY-MM-DD; for Hijri ⇄ Gregorian conversion use calc_hijri. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/date-diff/

InputTypeDescription
mode"difference" | "add"
default "difference"		difference between two dates, or add/subtract an amount.
startstringdifference: start date YYYY-MM-DD (default today).
endstringdifference: end date YYYY-MM-DD (default today).
inclusiveboolean
default false		difference: count the end date too.
weekend"fri-sat" | "sat-sun" | "fri" | "sun"
default "fri-sat"		Weekend days skipped by working-day counts.
datestringadd: the starting date YYYY-MM-DD (default today).
amountintegeradd: how many units to add (use subtract to go back).
unit"days" | "business_days" | "weeks" | "months" | "years"
default "days"		add: unit of amount.
subtractboolean
default false		add: go back in time instead.

Example: 5 days, 5 working days

{

  "end": "2026-10-31",
  "weekend": "sat-sun"
}

  "wholeMonths": 0,
  "workingDays": 5,
  "weekendDays": 0,
  "endBeforeStart": false,
  "summary": "From Monday, 26 October 2026 to Saturday, 31 October 2026: 5 days (5 days), 5 working days with Sat–Sun weekends"
}

calc_discountDiscount / sale price calculator

Three shop maths jobs. sale: price after one or two stacked discounts (each a percent or a fixed amount) plus optional tax/VAT, with what you save and the effective total discount (stacked percents multiply). reverse: the discount % between an old and a new price. compare: which of two deals is cheaper per unit (price and amount of each). Money is rounded to the cent at each step like a till. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/discount-calc/

InputTypeDescription

default "sale"		Which calculator.

default "percent"		sale: first discount is a percent or a fixed amount.

extraDiscountType"percent" | "amount"
default "percent"		sale: second discount type.
taxPercentnumber | stringsale: optional tax / VAT percent added after the discounts.
wasnumber | stringreverse: the old price.
nownumber | stringreverse: the new price.
unitstringcompare: the unit name (kg, L, item…). Default "unit".
priceAnumber | stringcompare: price of deal A.
amountAnumber | stringcompare: amount of deal A (e.g. 0.5 for 500 g when unit is kg).
priceBnumber | stringcompare: price of deal B.
amountBnumber | stringcompare: amount of deal B.

calc_due_datePregnancy due date

Estimated pregnancy due date from the first day of the last period (Naegele's rule, adjusted for cycle length 20–45 days), the conception date, or an IVF embryo transfer date (day-3 or day-5 embryo). Also gives the estimated conception date, full-term date, how far along you are on a given day (weeks + days, trimester, days to go) and a dated timeline of common milestones (weeks 4–42), optionally with Hijri dates. Estimate only, not medical advice. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/due-date/

InputTypeDescription

cycleLengthinteger

default 5		ivf: embryo age at transfer, day 3 or day 5.
todaystringWork out progress on this date, YYYY-MM-DD (default today).
hijriboolean
default false		Also give Umm al-Qura Hijri dates.

calc_equationEquation solver

Solve equations exactly with worked steps, like the website: linear a·x + b = c, quadratic a·x² + b·x + c = 0 (exact rational or surd roots like 1/2 + √5/2, complex roots), cubic a·x³ + b·x² + c·x + d = 0 (rational root test, else trigonometric/Cardano to 10 significant digits), and systems of 2 or 3 linear equations (Gauss–Jordan; no solution and infinitely many solutions are detected and parametrised). Coefficients may be integers, decimals, fractions ("3/4"), mixed numbers ("-1 1/2") or e-notation. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/equation-solver/

InputTypeDescription
type required"linear" | "quadratic" | "cubic" | "system"Equation type.
coefficients(number | string)[]linear: [a, b, c] for a·x + b = c; quadratic: [a, b, c]; cubic: [a, b, c, d].
system((number | string)[])[]system: rows of the augmented matrix, 2 rows of [a, b, rhs] (x, y) or 3 rows of [a, b, c, rhs] (x, y, z).

Example: x = 1/2 ± √5/2

{
  "type": "quadratic",
  "coefficients": [
    1,
    -1,
    -1
  ]
}

    }

}

Evaluate a maths expression safely with the website's own parser (never eval): + − × ÷ ^ (right-associative), implicit multiplication (2(3+4), 2x), postfix ! and %, √, brackets; functions sin cos tan sec csc cot (+ asin acos atan atan2) in degrees (default), radians or grads, sinh cosh tanh and inverses, sqrt cbrt root(x,n) abs exp ln log(x[,base]) log10 log2 floor ceil round sign min max mod nCr nPr fact gamma; constants pi π e tau phi; "ans" and your own variables. Results to 12 significant digits, plain or scientific notation. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/sci-calc/

InputTypeDescription

angle"deg" | "rad" | "grad"
default "deg"		Angle unit for trig functions.
variablesobjectVariables to use, e.g. {"x": 2, "r": 5}.
ansnumberValue of "ans" (the previous answer). Default 0.
format"norm" | "sci"
default "norm"		Display: normal, or always scientific notation.

}

}

calc_fuel_costFuel cost of a trip

Fuel needed and cost of a trip from distance (km or miles, optionally round trip), fuel price (per litre, US gallon or UK gallon) and consumption in L/100 km, km/L, mpg (US) or mpg (UK); split between people; cost per km/mile and per 100. Optionally compare a second car (its own consumption and, if different, fuel price). Also converts the consumption to the other units. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/fuel-cost/

InputTypeDescription
distance requirednumber | stringTrip distance (one way unless roundTrip).
distanceUnit"km" | "mi"
default "km"		Distance unit.
roundTripboolean
default false		Double the distance (there and back).
price requirednumber | stringFuel price per priceUnit.
priceUnit"L" | "galUS" | "galUK"
default "L"		The price is per litre, US gallon or UK gallon.
consumption requirednumber | stringCar consumption / economy, e.g. 7.5 (L/100 km) or 35 (mpg).
consumptionUnit"l100" | "kml" | "mpgus" | "mpguk"
default "l100"		l100 = L/100 km, kml = km/L, mpgus, mpguk.
peopleinteger
default 1		Split the cost between this many people.
currencystringCurrency label for the text, e.g. "JD" or "{{body}}quot;.
namestringName of the car (default "Car A").
compareobjectOptional second car to compare.

Example: 70.35 L, cost 63.32, 21.11 each

{
  "distance": 335,
  "roundTrip": true,
  "price": 0.9,

calc_hijriHijri ⇄ Gregorian converter

Convert between Gregorian and Hijri dates with the Umm al-Qura calendar (the same Intl calendar the website uses), both ways. Modes: to_hijri (a Gregorian date), to_gregorian (Hijri year, month 1–12, day), month (length of a Hijri month and its Gregorian start/end), age (age in Hijri and Gregorian years, next Hijri birthday). Results come in English and Arabic (Arabic-Indic digits by default; Gregorian month names in the Egyptian/Gulf or Levant style). Supported range 1300–1600 AH (1882–2174). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/hijri-converter/

calc_split_billSplit a bill

Split a restaurant bill with tax/service and tip, in whole cents so the shares always add up exactly (left-over cents go to the largest remainders). simple: one bill split evenly between N people (1–100). items: list who had what; each item is split between the people who shared it (nobody listed = everyone), and tax and tip are shared in proportion. Tip can be on the subtotal or on subtotal + tax; each share can be rounded up to a step (e.g. 0.5 or 1). Returns a ready-to-send summary. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/split-bill/

InputTypeDescription

default "simple"		Even split, or itemised.

itemsobject[]items: what was ordered.
taxPercentnumber
default 0		Tax / service charge percent.
tipPercentnumber
default 10		Tip percent (default 10).
tipOnTaxboolean
default false		Calculate the tip on subtotal + tax.
roundUpTonumber
default 0		Round each share up to a multiple of this (e.g. 0.5, 1, 5). 0 = exact.
currencystringCurrency, e.g. "JOD" (written after) or "{{body}}quot; (written before).

calc_statsDescriptive statistics

Descriptive statistics for a list of numbers: count, sum, mean, median, mode(s), min, max, range, sample and population variance and SD, standard error, coefficient of variation, quartiles (inclusive = Excel QUARTILE.INC, exclusive = QUARTILE.EXC, or Tukey median-of-halves), IQR, 1.5×IQR outlier fences and outliers, a frequency table (values, or Sturges bins when there are more than 20 distinct values) and z-scores. Paste numbers separated by spaces, commas, semicolons, | or new lines; headers and text are skipped and reported. Commas can be separators, thousands marks or decimal commas. Up to 1,000,000 numbers. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/stats-calc/

InputTypeDescription
data requiredstring | number[]The numbers: a text blob (CSV column, list…) or an array of numbers.
commas"sep" | "thou" | "dec"
default "sep"		In text: commas separate values (sep), are thousands marks (thou: 1,234), or decimal commas (dec: 1,5).
quartileMethod"inc" | "exc" | "tukey"
default "inc"		Quartile method.
frequencyboolean
default true		Include the frequency table.
zScoresboolean
default false		Include a z-score for each value (first 1,000).

Example: mean 5, median 4.5, mode 4, population SD 2, sample SD 2.138

{
  "data": "2, 4, 4, 4, 5, 5, 7, 9"
}

calc_unit_convertUnit converter

Convert between units in 14 categories, the same table as the website: length, mass, temperature, volume, area (incl. dunam), speed, time, data (kB/MB vs KiB/MiB, bits), pressure, energy, power, angle, fuel economy (L/100km, km/L, mpg US/UK) and cooking (tsp, tbsp, cups, metric spoons). Give value + from + to (symbols like "km", "°F", "GiB" or names like "miles"; ASCII forms like "m2", "um" work), or a query like "5 km to mi". Set all=true for the value in every unit of the category, or list=true to see every category and unit. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/unit-convert/

InputTypeDescription
querystringA whole question, e.g. "5 km to mi" or "72 f in c".
valuenumber | stringThe number to convert.
fromstringUnit to convert from (symbol or name).
tostringUnit to convert to (symbol or name). Optional when all=true.
category"length" | "mass" | "temp" | "volume" | "area" | "speed" | "time" | "data" | "pressure" | "energy" | "power" | "angle" | "fuel" | "cooking"Category, to pick the right unit when a symbol exists in two (e.g. "pt" in volume and cooking).
allboolean
default false		Also return the value in every unit of the category.
listboolean
default false		Just list every category and its units.

Example: 3.106855961 mi

{
  "query": "5 km to mi"
}

Result

{
  "category": "length",
  "from": {
    "symbol": "km",
    "name": "kilometre"
  },
  "value": 5,
  "to": {
    "symbol": "mi",
    "name": "mile"
  },
  "result": 3.10685596119,
  "formatted": "3.106855961 mi",
  "formula": "5 km = 3.106855961 mi",
  "oneUnit": "1 km = 0.6213711922 mi"
}

Example

{
  "value": 100,
  "from": "°C",
  "to": "°F"
}

Result

{
  "result": 212
}
calc_zakatZakat calculator

Zakat on wealth: 2.5 % of net zakatable wealth (cash, bank, investments, trade goods, money owed to you, gold and silver, minus debts due now) when it reaches the nisab: the value of 85 g of pure gold (default) or 595 g of silver. Gold weight is converted to pure gold by karat (24/22/21/18). You give today's gold/silver price per gram in your currency: nothing is fetched from the network. An estimate only; consult a scholar. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/zakat-calc/

InputTypeDescription
cashnumber | stringCash in hand.
banknumber | stringBank balances.
investmentsnumber | stringShares, funds and other investments.
tradeGoodsnumber | stringValue of goods held for sale.
receivablesnumber | stringMoney owed to you that you expect back.
debtsnumber | stringDebts you must pay now (deducted).
goldGramsnumber | stringGold you own, in grams.
goldKarat24 | 22 | 21 | 18
default 21		Karat of that gold (default 21).
goldPricePerGramnumber | stringPrice of 1 g of pure (24k) gold. Needed for the gold nisab and to value gold.
silverGramsnumber | stringSilver you own, in grams.
silverPricePerGramnumber | stringPrice of 1 g of silver. Needed for the silver nisab and to value silver.
nisabBasis"gold" | "silver"
default "gold"		Which metal sets the nisab.
currencystringCurrency label for the summary (e.g. JOD).

Example: net 18250, nisab 5100, zakat 456.25

{
  "cash": 5000,
  "bank": 10000,
  "goldGrams": 100,
  "goldKarat": 21,
  "goldPricePerGram": 60,
  "debts": 2000
}

Result

{
  "pureGoldGrams": 87.5,
  "goldValue": 5250,
  "silverValue": 0,
  "totalAssets": 20250,
  "netWealth": 18250,
  "nisabBasis": "gold",
  "nisab": 5100,
  "due": true,
  "zakat": 456.25,
  "message": "456.25 is due: 2.5% of 18,250.00, above the nisab of 5,100.00.",
  "summary": "Zakat calculation\nNet zakatable wealth: 18,250.00\nNisab (gold): 5,100.00\nZakat due (2.5%): 456.25\n(Estimate only; consult a scholar.)"
}

Everyday & security

Prayer times, qibla, world clock, random picks, Secret Santa, passwords, encryption.

crypto_file_decryptDecrypt a file (.s3enc)

Decrypt an .s3enc file made by the S3eidat File Encrypt website or crypto_file_encrypt, verify every chunk, and write the original file back under its stored (sanitised) name. A wrong password, damage, truncation or reordering is reported and nothing is written. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/file-encrypt/

InputTypeDescription
file requiredstringThe .s3enc file: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
password requiredstringThe password. Normalised to Unicode NFC first, like the website.
outputstringWhere to write the decrypted file: a file path, or a folder ending in "/" (keeps the stored name). Default: the server output folder.
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example: Writes report.pdf

{
  "file": "/path/to/report.pdf.s3enc",
  "password": "correct horse battery staple"
}
crypto_file_encryptEncrypt a file (.s3enc)

Encrypt any file with a password into the website's .s3enc format: chunked AES-256-GCM, key from PBKDF2-SHA256 (600,000 rounds, random salt), original name/type/size stored encrypted inside, tamper/truncation/reorder-proof. Files made here open at https://s3eidat.github.io/file-encrypt/ and vice versa (crypto_file_decrypt). Whole file is processed in memory (size limit = server --max-mb). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/file-encrypt/

InputTypeDescription
file requiredstringThe file to encrypt: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
password requiredstringThe password. Normalised to Unicode NFC first, like the website.
namestringFile name to store inside (restored on decrypt). Default: the input file name.
mimeTypestringMIME type to store. Default: guessed from the extension like a browser would ("" if unknown).
chunkSizeinteger
default 4194304		Chunk size in bytes (website: 4 MiB).
iterationsinteger
default 600000		PBKDF2 rounds (website: 600,000).
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example: Writes report.pdf.s3enc

{
  "file": "/path/to/report.pdf",
  "password": "correct horse battery staple"
}
crypto_stego_hideHide a message in an image

Hide a text message inside an image (LSB steganography in R, G, B; the picture looks the same) and save it as a lossless PNG, optionally encrypted with a password (AES-256-GCM, PBKDF2-SHA256 600,000). Same bit layout as the website, so the PNG reveals at https://s3eidat.github.io/stego-image/ or with crypto_stego_reveal. Capacity ≈ width×height×3/8 − 10 bytes (−44 more when encrypted). Transparency is filled with white. The result is read back and checked before returning. Sending it through apps that recompress images (JPEG, chat apps) destroys the message. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/stego-image/

InputTypeDescription
image requiredstringThe cover image (PNG, JPEG, WebP…): a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
message requiredstringThe text to hide (UTF-8).
passwordstringOptional password to encrypt the message.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example: Writes photo-hidden.png

{
  "image": "/path/to/photo.jpg",
  "message": "Meet at the old library at 7.",
  "password": "correct horse battery staple"
}
crypto_stego_revealReveal a hidden message

Read a message hidden in a PNG by the S3eidat Stego Image website or crypto_stego_hide (decrypting it if a password was used). Reports when there is no message, e.g. because the image was recompressed (JPEG), resized or edited. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/stego-image/

InputTypeDescription
image requiredstringThe image with a hidden message (PNG): a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
passwordstringPassword, if the message is encrypted.

Example

{
  "image": "/path/to/photo-hidden.png",
  "password": "correct horse battery staple"
}
crypto_text_decryptDecrypt text (AES-256-GCM)

Decrypt an "S3E1.…" message made by the S3eidat Text Encrypt website or crypto_text_encrypt, with its password. Verifies integrity: a wrong password or any change to the text is reported, never garbage output. Accepts base64url and line breaks. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/text-encrypt/

InputTypeDescription
encryptedstringThe encrypted text, starting with S3E1.
pathstringOr: a file containing it (e.g. encrypted.txt).
password requiredstringThe password. Normalised to Unicode NFC first, like the website.
outputstringOptional: also save the decrypted message to this file.

Example

{
  "encrypted": "S3E1.AQEACSfAtcIyfKiQlnccWg4e2uFxBglGUizyF0IsTYNU0kSqKpIDX4xsscpbHaTSv0AGmLwAcP3l341prQNNoeukuzER22r7",
  "password": "s3eidat"
}

Result

{
  "text": "Hello, مرحبا 👋",
  "iterations": 600000
}
crypto_text_encryptEncrypt text (AES-256-GCM)

Encrypt a message with a password: AES-256-GCM, key from PBKDF2-SHA256 (600,000 rounds, random 16-byte salt, 12-byte IV), output as one "S3E1.…" base64 line. Exactly the website's format, so the result can be decrypted at https://s3eidat.github.io/text-encrypt/ or with crypto_text_decrypt. Each run gives a different output. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/text-encrypt/

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe message to encrypt.
pathstringOr: a UTF-8 text file to encrypt.
password requiredstringThe password. Normalised to Unicode NFC first, like the website.
iterationsinteger
default 600000		PBKDF2 rounds (website: 600,000).
outputstringOptional: also save the encrypted line to this file (website "Save" gives encrypted.txt).

Example: → "S3E1.AQEACSfA…"

{
  "text": "Meet at the old library, 7 pm.",
  "password": "correct horse battery staple"
}
password_checkPassword strength check

Estimate how strong a password really is, the way attackers guess (zxcvbn-style): finds common passwords (1,282-entry list), dictionary words (also reversed, capitalised or l33t), keyboard walks, sequences, repeats, years and dates, then the cheapest way to guess the whole thing. Returns a 0–4 score, entropy bits, guesses, crack time for 4 attack scenarios, the pieces found, and advice. Offline only: no breach lookups, nothing stored or logged. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/password-check/

InputTypeDescription
password requiredstringThe password to check (only the first 256 characters are analysed in detail).
showPiecesboolean
default true		Include the matched pieces (they contain parts of the password).

Example: Weak: dictionary word + year + "!"

{
  "password": "Summer2024!"
}

password_generatePassword & passphrase generator

Generate strong random passwords (8–128 chars; lower/upper/digits/symbols; skip look-alikes Il1|O0o; exclude your own characters; require every chosen set) or passphrases (3–12 words from the website's 2,381-word list, separator, capitalise, add a number). Returns 1–1000 results with exact entropy bits, a strength grade and the time to crack at 100 billion guesses/s. Always crypto random (OS CSPRNG, rejection sampling); nothing is stored or logged. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/pass-forge/

InputTypeDescription
mode"password" | "passphrase"
default "password"		Random characters or random words.
lengthinteger
default 20		password: number of characters.
lowerboolean
default true		password: include a–z.
upperboolean
default true		password: include A–Z.
digitsboolean
default true		password: include 0–9.
symbolsboolean
default true		password: include symbols !#$%&*+-=?@^_~()[]{}<>.,:;/|
avoidLookalikesboolean
default true		password: leave out I l 1 | O 0 o.
requireEachSetboolean
default true		password: at least one character from every chosen set.
excludestring
default ""		password: characters never to use.
wordsinteger
default 6		passphrase: number of words.
separator"-" | " " | "." | "_" | ""
default "-"		passphrase: between words ("" = none, words then capitalised).
capitalizeboolean
default false		passphrase: capitalise each word.
addNumberboolean
default false		passphrase: append a number 0–99 to one random word.
countinteger
default 1		How many to generate.

Example: 20 chars from 83 possible, ≈127.3 bits, "Very strong"

{
  "length": 20
}

Example: e.g. "maple-orbit-velvet-harbor-pencil-river", ≈67.3 bits

{
  "mode": "passphrase",
  "words": 6,
  "separator": "-"
}
prayer_timesPrayer times

Islamic prayer times (Fajr, Sunrise, Dhuhr, Asr, Maghrib, Isha) for any coordinates + IANA time zone, or a known city, for one day, several days or a whole month, plus the Hijri (Umm al-Qura) date and the next prayer with a countdown. Same astronomy as the website (adhan.js): 13 methods (Jordan Awqaf, MWL, Umm al-Qura, Egyptian, ISNA, Karachi, Dubai, Kuwait, Qatar, Turkey, Singapore, Tehran, Moonsighting), Shafi'i or Hanafi Asr, and the recommended high-latitude rule beyond 48° (overridable). Times are local HH:MM (24 h) in the given zone, plus UTC ISO. Coordinates are rounded to 4 decimals like the site. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/prayer-times/

InputTypeDescription
citystringA known city (sets coordinates, time zone and its usual method): amman, irbid, zarqa, aqaba, mecca, medina, riyadh, jeddah, jerusalem, gaza, damascus, beirut, baghdad, cairo, alexandria, dubai, abudhabi, doha, kuwait, manama, muscat, istanbul, casablanca, tunis, algiers, khartoum, karachi, lahore, dhaka, delhi, kualalumpur, jakarta, tehran, london, paris, berlin, newyork, chicago, toronto, losangeles, sydney.
latitudenumberLatitude in degrees (−90..90). Use with longitude and timezone.
longitudenumberLongitude in degrees (−180..180).
timezonestringIANA time zone of the place, e.g. "Asia/Amman" (required with coordinates; overrides the city's).
datestringFirst day, YYYY-MM-DD in the place's calendar. Default: today there.
daysinteger
default 1		How many consecutive days to return.
monthstringOr a whole month timetable, YYYY-MM (overrides date/days).
method"jordan" | "mwl" | "ummalqura" | "egyptian" | "isna" | "karachi" | "dubai" | "kuwait" | "qatar" | "turkey" | "singapore" | "tehran" | "moonsighting"Calculation method. Default: the city's usual method, else "mwl".
asr"shafi" | "hanafi"Asr school: "shafi" (standard: Shafi'i, Maliki, Hanbali) or "hanafi". Default shafi (hanafi for Karachi-method cities).
highLatitudeRule"auto" | "middleofthenight" | "seventhofthenight" | "twilightangle"
default "auto"		"auto" = website behaviour (recommended rule when |latitude| > 48°).
nowstringReference instant (ISO 8601) for "today" and the next prayer. Default: the current time.

Example: Dhuhr 12:47 (09:47 UTC)

{
  "city": "amman",
  "date": "2026-01-15",
  "method": "mwl"
}
