qibla_directionQibla direction

Qibla direction (great-circle bearing from true north to the Kaaba in Makkah) and distance from any coordinates or a known city, with the compass point, a "face north, then turn right/left N°" instruction, and (if you give a device heading) how far to turn. Bearings are from TRUE north; a magnetic compass can differ by a few degrees. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/qibla-finder/

InputTypeDescription
latitudenumberLatitude in degrees (−90..90).
longitudenumberLongitude in degrees (−180..180).
citystringOr a known city: amman, irbid, zarqa, aqaba, mecca, medina, riyadh, jeddah, jerusalem, gaza, damascus, beirut, baghdad, cairo, alexandria, dubai, abudhabi, doha, kuwait, manama, muscat, istanbul, casablanca, tunis, algiers, khartoum, karachi, lahore, dhaka, delhi, kualalumpur, jakarta, tehran, london, paris, berlin, newyork, chicago, toronto, losangeles, sydney.
headingnumberOptional: the direction you are facing now (degrees from north) to get a turn instruction.

Example: Face north, then turn clockwise 160.7°

{
  "city": "amman"
}

Result

{
  "bearing": 160.7,
  "direction": "SSE",
  "distanceKm": 1234
}

Example: London: about 119° (ESE)

{
  "latitude": 51.5074,
  "longitude": -0.1278
}

Result

{
  "latitude": 51.5074,
  "longitude": -0.1278,
  "bearing": 119,
  "direction": "ESE",
  "directionAr": "جنوب شرق",
  "distanceKm": 4794,
  "atKaaba": false,
  "turnFromNorth": {
    "direction": "right",
    "degrees": 119
  },
  "instruction": "Face north, then turn clockwise (to your right) by 119°. You are then facing the Qibla (ESE)."
}
random_diceDice roller, coin flip, random numbers

Roll dice with standard notation (2d6+3, d20, d%, 4d6dl1 / "4d6 drop lowest", 2d20kh1 advantage, 2d20kl1, 5d10dh2; up to 100 dice per term, 2–1000 sides, 20 terms), flip coins (counts + streaks), or draw random whole numbers in a range (optionally unique and sorted, up to 10,000). Crypto random with rejection sampling (no modulo bias); optional seed for reproducible results. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/dice-roller/

InputTypeDescription
mode"dice" | "coin" | "numbers"
default "dice"		What to do.
notationstring
default "2d6"		dice: the dice notation, e.g. "3d8+2d6+4".
rollsinteger
default 1		dice: how many times to roll the expression.
flipsinteger
default 1		coin: how many coins to flip.
mininteger
default 1		numbers: smallest value (inclusive).
maxinteger
default 100		numbers: largest value (inclusive).
countinteger
default 1		numbers: how many numbers.
uniqueboolean
default false		numbers: no repeats.
sortboolean
default false		numbers: sort ascending.
seedstringOptional seed for a reproducible result (same seed + same input = same output). Omit for true crypto randomness.

Example: e.g. total 13, breakdown "4d6dl1 [3, (1 dropped), 4, 6] = 13"

{
  "notation": "4d6 drop lowest"
}

Example

{
  "mode": "coin",
  "flips": 10
}

Example: Lottery-style draw

{
  "mode": "numbers",
  "min": 1,
  "max": 49,
  "count": 6,
  "unique": true,
  "sort": true
}
random_pickWheel picker (random pick)

Pick a random winner (or several, without repeats, like spinning with "remove winner" on) from a list of names, or shuffle / sort the whole list. Fair: crypto random with rejection sampling. Entries are trimmed, cut to 100 characters, max 500; at least 2 needed. Optional seed for a reproducible draw. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/wheel-picker/

InputTypeDescription
itemsstring[]The entries, one per array item.
textstringOr: the entries as text, one per line.
mode"pick" | "shuffle" | "sort"
default "pick"		"pick" winners, "shuffle" the list, or "sort" it A→Z.
countinteger
default 1		How many winners to pick (each winner is removed before the next spin).
seedstringOptional seed for a reproducible result (same seed + same input = same output). Omit for true crypto randomness.

Example: One random winner, e.g. { winners: [{ name: "Omar", index: 2 }] }

{
  "items": [
    "Ali",
    "Sara",
    "Omar",
    "Lina"
  ]
}

Example: Three distinct winners, reproducible

{
  "text": "Ali\nSara\nOmar\nLina\nHuda",
  "count": 3,
  "seed": "raffle-2026"
}
random_teamsTeam maker

Split a list of people into fair random teams: choose the number of teams or people per team. Add a skill level ("Ali, 5" / "Sara: 3") and teams are balanced by total skill (people without one count as the average). Team names: numbers, letters, colours, animals or your own. Crypto-random shuffle; optional seed for a reproducible split. Max 2000 people, 100 teams. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/team-maker/

InputTypeDescription
peoplestring[]People, one per item, optionally "Name, skill" (skill 0–100).
textstringOr: people as text, one per line.
mode"teams" | "per"
default "teams"		"teams": make `num` teams; "per": `num` people per team.
numinteger
default 2		Number of teams (min 2) or people per team (min 1), see mode.
balanceboolean
default true		Balance teams by skill when skills are given.
naming"num" | "letters" | "colors" | "animals" | "custom"
default "num"		Team names: Team 1…, Team A…, colours, animals, or customNames.
customNamesstring[]naming=custom: team names in order (missing ones fall back to "Team N").
seedstringOptional seed for a reproducible result (same seed + same input = same output). Omit for true crypto randomness.

Example: Two balanced teams (Lions, Falcons)

{
  "people": [
    "Ali, 5",
    "Sara, 4",
    "Omar, 3",
    "Lina, 5",
    "Yousef, 2",
    "Noor, 4"
  ],
  "num": 2,
  "naming": "animals"
}
secret_santa_drawSecret Santa draw

Draw Secret Santa (gift exchange) pairs: everyone gives to exactly one other person and receives from one, never themselves. Exclusions (couples, housemates…) block a pair both ways. Checks exactly whether a draw is possible and explains why not (and which exclusion to drop). Crypto random and uniform; optional seed. Up to 200 people. Nothing is emailed: hand each giver only their own line. Optional budget, date and message are echoed for slips. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/secret-santa/

InputTypeDescription
names requiredstring[]Participants (an item may also hold several names separated by commas or new lines).
exclusionsany[][]
default []		Pairs of names who must not draw each other, e.g. [["Omar","Sara"]].
budgetstringOptional gift budget, e.g. "20 JD".
datestringOptional exchange date, YYYY-MM-DD.
messagestringOptional note for everyone.
seedstringOptional seed for a reproducible result (same seed + same input = same output). Omit for true crypto randomness.

Example: Omar and Sara never draw each other

{
  "names": [
    "Omar",
    "Sara",
    "Lina",
    "Yousef",
    "Huda"
  ],
  "exclusions": [
    [
      "Omar",
      "Sara"
    ]
  ],
  "budget": "20 JD"
}
time_world_clockWorld clock & meeting planner

Current (or any given) time in a list of time zones or cities: local time, date, weekday, UTC offset, difference from a home zone, day/night and working-hours flags. Plus the website's meeting planner: the 24 hours of a day in the home zone, each zone's local hour and band (work 9–17, awake 7–22, night), and the hours when everyone is in working hours. Zones: IANA ids ("Asia/Tokyo") or city names ("Mecca", "New York", "Tokyo"). Uses Node's ICU time-zone data; no network. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/world-clock/

InputTypeDescription
zones requiredstring[]Time zones or cities, e.g. ["Asia/Amman", "London", "New York"]. The first is the home zone unless homeZone is set.
homeZonestringZone the differences and the planner day are measured from. Default: the first of zones.
atstringThe instant to show (ISO 8601, e.g. "2026-01-15T12:00:00Z"). Default: now.
hour12boolean
default false		12-hour clock (AM/PM) instead of 24-hour.
plannerboolean
default true		Include the 24-hour meeting planner.
plannerDatestringPlanner day (YYYY-MM-DD in the home zone). Default: the date of "at" there.

Example: Working-hours overlap 14:00–16:00 UTC (hours 14, 15, 16)

{
  "zones": [
    "Europe/London",
    "America/New_York"
  ],
  "at": "2026-01-15T12:00:00Z"
}

QR & barcodes

Make and read QR codes, Wi-Fi QR, barcodes.

barcode_generateBarcode generator

Make 1D barcodes as SVG or PNG: Code 128 (any printable ASCII; digit runs packed with subset C automatically), EAN-13 (12 digits, check digit added; or 13, check digit verified), UPC-A (11 or 12 digits, same) and Code 39 (A-Z, 0-9, space, - . $ / + %, optional mod-43 check). Same encoders, quiet zones, guard bars and human-readable digits as the website. Give "value" for one barcode, or "values" (up to 1000) to write one file per value into a folder; invalid values are reported, not fatal. PNG text uses the system monospace font. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/barcode-gen/

InputTypeDescription
valuestringThe value to encode (one barcode).
valuesstring[]Or: many values; each becomes its own file in the output folder.
symbology"code128" | "ean13" | "upca" | "code39"
default "code128"		Barcode type (default code128).
code39Checkboolean
default false		Code 39 only: append the mod-43 check character.
format"svg" | "png"
default "svg"		Output format (default svg).
barWidthinteger
default 2		Narrow bar (module) width in px, 1-6 (default 2).
heightinteger
default 80		Bar height in px, 30-220 (default 80).
showTextboolean
default true		Print the human-readable text/digits under the bars.
foregroundstring
default "#000000"		Bar colour, hex.
backgroundstring
default "#ffffff"		Background colour, hex.
scaleinteger
default 2		PNG only: pixel scale (1×, 2× default, 4×…).
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example

{
  "value": "400638133393",
  "symbology": "ean13"
}

Result

{
  "text": "4006381333931",
  "note": "Check digit 1 added"
}

Example: Code 128 SVG

{
  "value": "S3EIDAT-2026"
}

Result

{
  "path": "C:\\Users\\User\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\s3eidat-docs-npITvj\\S3EIDAT-2026.svg",
  "bytes": 2214,
  "mimeType": "image/svg+xml",
  "text": "S3EIDAT-2026",
  "width": 352,
  "height": 123,
  "modules": 156
}

Example: Two files, one error (12345 is too short).

{
  "values": [
    "590123412345",
    "400638133393",
    "12345"
  ],
  "symbology": "ean13",
  "format": "png"
}

Result

{
  "folder": "C:\\Users\\User\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\s3eidat-docs-dH0Pbu\\barcodes-ean13\\",
  "files": [
    {
      "value": "590123412345",
      "path": "C:\\Users\\User\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\s3eidat-docs-dH0Pbu\\barcodes-ean13\\5901234123457.png",
      "text": "5901234123457"
    },
    {
      "value": "400638133393",
      "path": "C:\\Users\\User\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\s3eidat-docs-dH0Pbu\\barcodes-ean13\\4006381333931.png",
      "text": "4006381333931"
    }
  ],
  "errors": [
    {
      "line": 3,
      "value": "12345",
      "error": "EAN-13 needs 12 or 13 digits (you have 5)"
    }
  ]
}
qr_generateQR code generator

Make a QR code as SVG or PNG for a link, plain text, Wi-Fi login, contact card (vCard 3.0), email, SMS, phone call or map location. Same encoder as the website (qrcode.js: versions 1-40, error correction L/M/Q/H, smallest version that fits, best mask), UTF-8 so Arabic/emoji work. Options: size in px, quiet-zone margin, colours, square/rounded/dots modules, and an optional centre logo (forces level H). Returns the file path, the exact encoded text, version, module count and contrast warnings. Max about 2.9 KB of data at level L. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/qr-studio/

InputTypeDescription
type"url" | "text" | "wifi" | "vcard" | "email" | "sms" | "tel" | "geo"What the code holds. Default: "url" if url is given, else "text".
textstringtext: any text (encoded exactly as given).
urlstringurl: web address; "https://" is added when no scheme is given.
ssidstringwifi: network name.
passwordstringwifi: password (ignored for open networks).
security"WPA" | "WEP" | "nopass"wifi: WPA (WPA/WPA2/WPA3, default), WEP, or nopass (open).
hiddenbooleanwifi: the network is hidden.
firstNamestringvcard: first name.
lastNamestringvcard: last name.
phonestringvcard/sms/tel: phone number.
emailstringvcard: email address.
companystringvcard: company.
jobTitlestringvcard: job title.
websitestringvcard: website.
addressstringvcard: address.
tostringemail: recipient address.
subjectstringemail: subject.
bodystringemail: message body.
messagestringsms: message text.
latitudenumber | stringgeo: latitude, -90..90.
longitudenumber | stringgeo: longitude, -180..180.
labelstringgeo: optional place label (?q=).
ecc"L" | "M" | "Q" | "H"
default "M"		Error correction: L 7%, M 15% (default), Q 25%, H 30%. A logo forces H.
mode"byte" | "auto" | "numeric" | "alphanumeric"
default "byte"		Encoding mode. "byte" (UTF-8, what the website uses), "auto" (numeric/alphanumeric when the text allows, smaller codes), or force one.
versioninteger
default 0		QR version 1-40 (size), or 0 = smallest that fits (default).
format"svg" | "png"
default "png"		Output format.
sizeinteger
default 1024		Image width/height in px (default 1024).
margininteger
default 4		Quiet zone in modules (default 4; under 2 may not scan).
foregroundstring
default "#0d0f12"		Module colour, hex (default #0d0f12).
backgroundstring
default "#ffffff"		Background colour, hex (default #ffffff).
shape"square" | "rounded" | "dots"
default "square"		Module shape. Rounded/dots also round the three finder squares.
logostringOptional centre logo image (path or base64, max 5 MB). Covers ~22% of the code; error correction becomes H.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example: Encodes "https://example.com" as a 1024 px PNG.

{
  "url": "example.com"
}

Result

{
  "path": "C:\\Users\\User\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\s3eidat-docs-Y92THC\\qr-url.png",
  "bytes": 17533,
  "mimeType": "image/png",
  "payload": "https://example.com",
  "version": 2,
  "modules": 25,
  "ecc": "M",
  "mode": "Byte",
  "mask": 5,
  "dataBytes": 19,
  "contrast": 19.19,
  "warnings": []
}

Example: payload WIFI:T:WPA;S:Home;P:12345678;;

{
  "type": "wifi",
  "ssid": "Home",
  "password": "12345678",
  "format": "svg"
}

Result

{
  "path": "C:\\Users\\User\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\s3eidat-docs-SUmYUZ\\qr-wifi.svg",
  "bytes": 5788,
  "mimeType": "image/svg+xml",
  "payload": "WIFI:T:WPA;S:Home;P:12345678;;",
  "version": 3,
  "modules": 29,
  "ecc": "M",
  "mode": "Byte",
  "mask": 1,
  "dataBytes": 30,
  "contrast": 19.19,
  "warnings": []
}

Example

{
  "type": "vcard",
  "firstName": "Sara",
  "lastName": "Haddad",
  "phone": "+962 7 9000 0000",
  "shape": "rounded"
}

Result

{
  "path": "C:\\Users\\User\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\s3eidat-docs-nWOSYW\\qr-vcard.png",
  "bytes": 138314,
  "mimeType": "image/png",
  "payload": "BEGIN:VCARD\r\nVERSION:3.0\r\nN:Haddad;Sara;;;\r\nFN:Sara Haddad\r\nTEL;TYPE=CELL:+962 7 9000 0000\r\nEND:VCARD",
  "version": 6,
  "modules": 41,
  "ecc": "M",
  "mode": "Byte",
  "mask": 4,
  "dataBytes": 101,
  "contrast": 19.19,
  "warnings": []

qr_readQR code reader

Read (decode) a QR code from an image file: photo, screenshot or scan (PNG, JPEG, WebP, GIF, TIFF, AVIF, SVG). Uses jsQR like the website, trying several sizes and inverted (light-on-dark) codes. Returns the text plus what it is (Link, Wi-Fi, Contact, Email, SMS, Phone, Location, Event or Text) with parsed fields, and safety notes for links (http, hidden @ user part, punycode lookalikes, bare IPs, odd ports). Links are never opened. Reads one code per image. Web version (with camera): https://s3eidat.github.io/qr-scanner/

InputTypeDescription
image requiredstringThe image containing a QR code: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).

Example: e.g. { text: "WIFI:T:WPA;S:Home;P:secret;;", type: "Wi-Fi", fields: [{label:"Network",value:"Home"},…] }

{
  "image": "/path/to/photo-of-qr.jpg"
}
qr_wifiWi-Fi QR code and card

Make a Wi-Fi QR code that phones join by scanning (WPA/WPA2/WPA3, WEP or open; hidden networks), as PNG or SVG, or a printable PDF card like the website's: coloured header with the Wi-Fi symbol, "Scan to join", the QR code, network name and (optionally) the password, on an A6 card, an A4 poster, or A4 sheets of 4 or 9 cards to cut. Special characters (; , : " \) are escaped. The PDF card uses English labels and Latin text only for now. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/wifi-card/

InputTypeDescription
ssid requiredstringNetwork name (SSID).
passwordstring
default ""		Wi-Fi password (ignored for open networks).
security"WPA" | "WEP" | "nopass"
default "WPA"		WPA (WPA/WPA2/WPA3, default), WEP, or nopass (open network).
hiddenboolean
default false		The network is hidden (not broadcast).
format"png" | "svg" | "pdf"
default "pdf"		pdf = printable card sheet (default); png/svg = just the QR code.
layout"a6" | "a4" | "a4x4" | "a4x9"
default "a4x4"		PDF: a6 = one A6 card; a4 = one A4 poster; a4x4 = A4 sheet of 4 cards (default); a4x9 = A4 sheet of 9 small cards.
theme"teal" | "ink" | "coral" | "violet" | "saver"
default "teal"		PDF: card colour; "saver" is an ink-saving outline.
titlestring
default ""		PDF: heading on the card (default "Wi-Fi").
showPasswordboolean
default true		PDF: print the password under the code.
sizeinteger
default 1024		PNG/SVG: image size in px.
margininteger
default 4		PNG/SVG: quiet zone in modules.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example: A4 sheet with 4 cards; payload WIFI:T:WPA;S:S3eidat Café;P:coffee-is-free;;

{
  "ssid": "S3eidat Café",
  "password": "coffee-is-free"
}

Result

{
  "path": "C:\\Users\\User\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\s3eidat-docs-jx8uxA\\wifi-card.pdf",
  "bytes": 6360,
  "mimeType": "application/pdf",
  "pages": 1,
  "payload": "WIFI:T:WPA;S:S3eidat Café;P:coffee-is-free;;",
  "version": 4,
  "modules": 33,
  "warnings": []
}

Example: payload WIFI:T:nopass;S:Guest;;

{
  "ssid": "Guest",
  "security": "nopass",
  "format": "png"
}

Result

{
  "path": "C:\\Users\\User\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\s3eidat-docs-REoI5U\\wifi-qr.png",
  "bytes": 17598,
  "mimeType": "image/png",
  "payload": "WIFI:T:nopass;S:Guest;;",
  "version": 2,
  "modules": 25,
  "warnings": []

Document makers

Invoices, receipts, business cards, labels, calendars and CVs as PDF.

doc_attendance_sheetAttendance sheet PDF

Make a printable attendance / sign-in sheet PDF: names down the side, one column per date for a month (skipping chosen weekdays, e.g. Fri+Sat or Sat+Sun), a run of N sessions from a start date, or N blank columns; tick boxes or wide signature cells; row numbers, a total column, extra empty rows, title, group and teacher. Same page maths as the website: rows that don't fit continue on more pages, and too many date columns are split across pages with the same names. Also returns a blank register CSV. English labels, Latin text only for now. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/attendance-sheet/

InputTypeDescription
names requiredstring | string[]Names, one per line (leading "1." numbering and anything after a tab are removed), or a list.
titlestring
default ""		Title (default "Attendance").
groupstring
default ""		Class / group, shown under the title.
teacherstring
default ""		Teacher, shown under the title.
mode"month" | "sessions" | "blank"
default "month"		month = every day of a month; sessions = N dates from a start date; blank = N unnamed columns.
monthstringmonth mode: YYYY-MM (default this month).
startstringsessions mode: first date YYYY-MM-DD (default today).
countinteger
default 12		sessions/blank: number of columns.
skipinteger[]
default [0,6]		Weekdays to skip, 0 = Sunday … 6 = Saturday (default [0, 6] = weekends Sat+Sun; use [5, 6] for Fri+Sat).
cell"box" | "sign"
default "box"		box = tick boxes, sign = wide cells for signatures.
paper"A4" | "Letter"
default "A4"
orientation"landscape" | "portrait"
default "landscape"
rowHeightnumberRow height in mm (default 8, or 13 for signature cells).
extraRowsinteger
default 2		Empty rows after the names.
numbersboolean
default true		Row number column.
totalboolean
default true		Total column.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example: 22 date columns, 1 page.

{
  "names": "Ahmad Khalil\nLina Haddad\nOmar Saleh",
  "group": "Grade 7 B · Science",
  "teacher": "Ms. Rana",
  "month": "2026-11",
  "skip": [
    5,
    6
  ]
}

Result

doc_business_cardsBusiness cards PDF

Design business cards and get a print-ready PDF sheet: cards centred on A4 or Letter with crop marks (10 per sheet for 85 × 55 mm EU or 3.5 × 2 in US cards), plus a back side page mirrored for long-edge duplex printing. Four front templates (classic, band, centered, minimal), backs with your logo, a "scan to save my contact" vCard QR code, or blank; six colour themes or your own accent/paper/ink colours; optional logo and a vCard QR on the front. Also returns the vCard text. Print at 100% / actual size. Latin text only for now (the website also does an Arabic side). Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/business-card/

InputTypeDescription
name requiredstringYour name.

default "logo"		Back side: logo/company on the accent colour, a contact QR code, or none.
qrFrontboolean
default false		Also put the contact QR code on the front.
theme"teal" | "navy" | "charcoal" | "forest" | "violet" | "mono"
default "teal"		Colour preset (navy = navy & gold).
accentstringOverride the accent colour.
paperColorstringOverride the card background colour.
inkstringOverride the text colour.
size"eu" | "us"
default "eu"		eu = 85 × 55 mm, us = 3.5 × 2 in.
sheet"A4" | "Letter"
default "A4"		Paper for the print sheet.
gapnumber
default 0		Gap between cards in mm (0 = shared cut lines).
sides"both" | "front" | "back"
default "both"		Which pages to include (both = front page then mirrored back page).
logostringOptional logo image: a PNG/JPEG/WebP/GIF/SVG file path or base64. Drawn as-is (keep it small).
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example: A4 sheet of 10 fronts + mirrored sheet of 10 QR backs.

  "qrVersion": 11

doc_calendarCalendar PDF

Make a printable calendar PDF: a month grid (one page per month, several months in a row if you like) or a whole year on one page, A4 or Letter, landscape or portrait. Week starts on Saturday (default, like the website), Sunday or Monday; optional Hijri (Umm al-Qura) dates; events (one-off or yearly, 7 colours) shown in the day cells, or as coloured days plus a legend in the year view; optional title. English names by default; other Latin-script locales (fr, de, es, tr…) use their own month/day names. Web version: https://s3eidat.github.io/calendar-maker/

InputTypeDescription
view"month" | "year"
default "month"		month = month grid pages, year = 12 months on one page.
yearintegerYear (default: this year).
monthintegerMonth 1-12 (month view; default: this month).
monthsinteger
default 1		Month view: how many consecutive months (one page each).
weekStart0 | 1 | 6
default 6		First day of the week: 6 = Saturday (default), 0 = Sunday, 1 = Monday.
hijriboolean

ai_chartChart to SVG/PNG

Render a chart to an SVG or PNG file, offline. Either give a simple description ({ type: bar|line|area|pie|scatter, data: rows, x, y, color?, title? }; y may be a list of fields for multi-series line/bar charts), or a full Vega-Lite (or Vega) spec in "spec" with the rows inline in data.values. Field types are inferred (numbers = quantitative, ISO dates = temporal, other = categories). Loading data or images from URLs or files is refused. Up to 50,000 rows. PNG is rasterised from the SVG at "scale" (default 2×).

InputTypeDescription
specobjectA full Vega-Lite spec (or Vega, if its $schema says so). Data must be inline (data.values). Use this or "type".
type"bar" | "line" | "area" | "pie" | "scatter"Simple chart type. Use with data, x and y.
dataobject[]Rows for the simple chart, e.g. [{"month":"Jan","sales":12}].
xstringField for the x axis (the category / slice label for pie).
ystring | string[]Numeric field for the y axis (slice size for pie). A list of fields draws one series per field.
colorstringOptional field that splits the data into coloured series.
titlestring
widthintegerPlot width in px (default 600; pie 360).
heightintegerPlot height in px (default 360).
format"svg" | "png"
default "svg"		Output format: svg (default) or png.
scalenumber
default 2		PNG only: pixel density multiplier (2 = twice the SVG size, sharp on high-DPI screens). Default 2.
backgroundstring
default "#ffffff"		Background colour. Default white; "transparent" for none.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example

{
  "type": "bar",
  "data": [
    {
      "month": "Jan",
      "sales": 12
    },
    {
      "month": "Feb",
      "sales": 19
    },
    {
      "month": "Mar",
      "sales": 7
    }
  ],
  "x": "month",
  "y": "sales",
  "title": "Sales"
}

Result

{
  "format": "svg"
}

Example

{
  "type": "pie",
  "data": [
    {
      "k": "A",
      "v": 3
    },
    {
      "k": "B",
      "v": 1
    }
  ],
  "x": "k",
  "y": "v",
  "format": "png"
}

Result

{
  "format": "png",
  "mimeType": "image/png"
}
ai_chunk_textChunk text for RAG

Split text, markdown or a text file into token-sized chunks for retrieval (RAG) and embeddings. Splits at markdown headings (ATX "#" headings, never inside code fences) and paragraphs first, then sentences (lines inside code blocks), and only cuts mid-sentence when a single sentence is longer than maxTokens. Consecutive chunks of the same section share about overlapTokens of text. Each chunk returns index, text, tokens, startChar/endChar (JavaScript string offsets, end exclusive, so text === source.slice(startChar, endChar) once CRLF line endings are normalised to \n) and the heading path it sits under. Tokens are counted with tiktoken (o200k_base by default; for Claude treat counts as estimates). Give "text" or "path" (file path or base64); give "output" to also write the chunks as JSONL (one JSON object per line).

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe text (inline).
pathstringOr: a UTF-8 text/markdown file, as a local path or base64 (raw or a data: URI).
maxTokensinteger
default 500		Largest chunk, in tokens. Default 500.
overlapTokensinteger
default 50		Tokens repeated from the end of the previous chunk (same section only). Default 50. Must be below maxTokens.
markdownboolean
default true		Treat "#" lines as section headings (default true). Set false for plain text.
encoding"o200k_base" | "cl100k_base"
default "o200k_base"		Tokenizer: "o200k_base" (GPT-4o / o-series, default) or "cl100k_base" (GPT-4 / GPT-3.5 / text-embedding-3).
outputstringAlso write the chunks as JSONL to this file (or folder ending in "/").
includeTextbooleanReturn each chunk's text inline. Default: true, or false when "output" is given.
maxChunksinteger
default 2000		Most chunks to return inline (the JSONL file always has all). Default 2000.

Example: Two chunks, headings ["Guide","Install"] and ["Guide","Use"].

{
  "text": "# Guide\n\n## Install\n\nRun npm install.\n\n## Use\n\nCall the tool.",
  "maxTokens": 100,
  "overlapTokens": 0
}

Result

{
  "chunkCount": 2,
  "totalTokens": 17,
  "encoding": "o200k_base",
  "maxTokens": 100,
  "overlapTokens": 0,
  "chunks": [
    {
      "index": 0,
      "tokens": 10,
      "startChar": 0,
      "endChar": 37,
      "headings": [
        "Guide",
        "Install"
      ],
      "text": "# Guide\n\n## Install\n\nRun npm install."
    },
    {
      "index": 1,
      "tokens": 7,
      "startChar": 39,
      "endChar": 61,
      "headings": [
        "Guide",
        "Use"
      ],
      "text": "## Use\n\nCall the tool."
    }
  ]
}

Example

{
  "path": "handbook.md",
  "maxTokens": 400,
  "overlapTokens": 40,
  "output": "chunks.jsonl"
}

ai_create_xlsxCreate an Excel workbook (.xlsx)

Build a real Excel .xlsx file from data: one or more sheets, each with optional column definitions (header, key, width, numFmt) and rows given as objects or arrays. Bold shaded header, frozen header row and filter buttons (both on by default), column widths fitted to the content, Excel number formats (raw codes like "#,##0.00" or aliases: integer, number, percent, currency, usd, eur, gbp, date, datetime, time, text), strings starting with "=" written as live formulas (=SUM(B2:B9), =B2*C2, cross-sheet refs), ISO date strings written as real dates, and an optional totals row (SUM/AVERAGE/COUNT/MIN/MAX formulas). Up to 50 sheets and 1,000,000 rows per sheet. Formulas are calculated by Excel when the file is opened. Writes to "output" (default "workbook.xlsx" in the output folder) and returns the path and a summary of each sheet.

InputTypeDescription
sheets requiredobject[]
parseDatesboolean
default true		Write ISO date strings as Excel dates (default true).
titlestringWorkbook title (file properties, and the default file name).
authorstringAuthor in the file properties.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example

{
  "sheets": [
    {
      "name": "Orders",
      "columns": [
        {
          "header": "Item"
        },
        {
          "header": "Qty",
          "numFmt": "integer"
        },
        {
          "header": "Price",
          "numFmt": "usd"
        },
        {
          "header": "Total",
          "numFmt": "usd"
        }
      ],
      "rows": [
        [
          "Pen",
          3,
          1.5,
          "=B2*C2"
        ],
        [
          "Book",
          1,
          12,
          "=B3*C3"
        ]
      ],
      "totalsRow": {
        "Qty": "sum",
        "Total": "sum"
      }
    }
  ]
}

Result

{
  "sheets": [
    {
      "name": "Orders",
      "rows": 2,
      "columns": [
        "Item",
        "Qty",
        "Price",
        "Total"
      ],
      "range": "A1:D4",
      "formulas": 4
    }
  ]
}
ai_csv_profileProfile a table (CSV, JSON, XLSX)

Profile a table to understand and clean it before analysis: row and column counts, and per column the inferred type (integer, number, boolean, date, datetime, string), non-null and null counts, unique count, top 5 values, sample values, min/max/mean/median/stddev for numbers, min/max for ISO dates, min/max length for text, plus data-quality warnings (mixed types, leading/trailing spaces, mostly missing, constant columns, duplicate rows, ragged headers). Input: "file" (local path or base64 of CSV, TSV, JSON array, JSONL or .xlsx, first sheet or "sheet") or inline "csv" / "json". Empty cells, "null" and "NULL" count as missing. Runs locally; nothing is uploaded.

InputTypeDescription

csvstringOr: CSV/TSV text inline (delimiter detected; first row is the header).
jsonstring | object[]Or: JSON inline: an array of objects (or of arrays, first one the header), as a value or as JSON/JSONL text.

format"auto" | "csv" | "tsv" | "json" | "jsonl" | "xlsx"
default "auto"		Force the file format (default "auto": by extension, then by content).

Example

}

  "columns": 2,
  "duplicateRows": 1
}
ai_detect_languageDetect language

Detect the language of a text with franc-min (82 languages with 8M+ speakers, ISO 639-3 codes) and the writing systems it uses (Latin, Arabic, Cyrillic, Han/CJK, Hangul, Devanagari, Greek, Hebrew, Thai…). Returns the top 3 candidates with scores (1 = best match, relative to the others), English names and ISO 639-1 codes where they exist, plus a share per script. Texts under 20 characters are "too short": only the script is reported. Works best on a sentence or more of one language; for mixed text the dominant script wins; long texts are judged on their first 100,000 characters. Hebrew is not among the languages (script only). Give "text" or "path" (file path or base64).

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe text (inline).
pathstringOr: a UTF-8 text/markdown file, as a local path or base64 (raw or a data: URI).
onlystring[]Restrict to these ISO 639-3 codes (e.g. ["arb","eng","fra"]).

Example

{
  "text": "The quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog near the river bank."
}

Result

{
  "language": "eng",
  "iso6391": "en",
  "script": "Latin"
}

Example

{
  "text": "مرحبا بكم في موقعنا، نتمنى لكم يوما سعيدا"
}

Result

{
  "language": "arb",
  "iso6391": "ar",
  "script": "Arabic"
}

Example

{
  "text": "Hi there"
}

Result

{
  "tooShort": true,
  "script": "Latin"
}

Draw a flowchart / graph diagram from a tiny text language and save it as SVG or PNG, laid out automatically (dagre). One statement per line: `direction: LR` (TB, LR, BT, RL), `title: My flow`, `A [shape=diamond, label="Ready?", color=#fde68a]` (shapes: box, round, diamond, circle, ellipse), edges `A -> B: label`, chains `A -> B -> C`, `-->` dashed, `==>` thick, `<->` both ends, `--` no arrow; quote names with spaces ("Start here" -> A); `\n` in labels breaks lines; # starts a comment. Or pass JSON { nodes: [{id, label, shape, color}], edges: [{from, to, label, style, arrow}] } as "graph". Up to 500 nodes and 2000 edges. Labels use Helvetica/Arial metrics (Latin text is measured best).

InputTypeDescription
sourcestringDiagram text in the DSL (or a JSON graph as text).

theme"light" | "dark"
default "light"
format"svg" | "png"
default "svg"		Output format: svg (default) or png.
scalenumber
default 2		PNG only: pixel density multiplier (2 = twice the SVG size, sharp on high-DPI screens). Default 2.

returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example

  "format": "svg",
  "nodes": 4,
  "edges": 4
}

        "id": "a",

ai_extract_entitiesExtract entities

Pull structured items out of text or a text file, deduplicated with counts: emails, URLs, phone numbers (libphonenumber, validated, E.164 plus type/country; local numbers read with defaultRegion, default JO), dates (ISO, D/M/Y or M/D/Y, Y/M/D, English month names; normalised to YYYY-MM-DD; a date like 03/04/2024 is flagged ambiguous with the other reading), money ($, €, £, JOD/JD/د.أ, SAR/ر.س, AED/د.إ, KWD, EGP… and Arabic currency words, with amount and ISO currency), percentages, #hashtags, @mentions, IPv4/IPv6, IBANs (country length and mod-97 checked) and card-like numbers (only Luhn-valid ones with a known brand prefix, always masked: only the last 4 digits are shown, the full number is never returned). Arabic-Indic digits are understood. Regex-based, not NLP: no names, places or organisations. Give "text" or "path" (file path or base64).

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe text (inline).
pathstringOr: a UTF-8 text/markdown file, as a local path or base64 (raw or a data: URI).
types("emails" | "urls" | "phones" | "dates" | "money" | "percentages" | "hashtags" | "mentions" | "ipv4" | "ipv6" | "ibans" | "cards")[]Only these types (default all): emails, urls, phones, dates, money, percentages, hashtags, mentions, ipv4, ipv6, ibans, cards.
defaultRegionstring
default "JO"		Two-letter country for local phone numbers, regional currencies (dinar, riyal…) and D/M/Y vs M/D/Y. Default JO.
maxPerTypeinteger
default 500		Most unique items to return per type. Default 500.

Example

{
  "text": "Mail omar@example.com or call 079 123 4567 before 15/03/2025. Fee: 25 JOD (10% off). #sale @s3eidat"
}

Result

{
  "counts": {
    "emails": 1,
    "phones": 1,
    "dates": 1,
    "money": 1,
    "percentages": 1,
    "hashtags": 1,
    "mentions": 1
  }
}
ai_file_identifyIdentify a file type

Find out what a file really is from its content (magic bytes, via file-type: images, video, audio, archives, Office, PDF, fonts, executables… ~150 formats), not its name. Reports the detected type and MIME, whether the extension disagrees with the content, size, SHA-256, and for text files an encoding guess (UTF-8, UTF-8/16/32 with BOM, UTF-16 without BOM, ASCII, 8-bit legacy) plus a text kind (JSON, XML, SVG, HTML…). Accepts a file path or base64. Nothing is executed.

InputTypeDescription
file requiredstringThe file to identify: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).

  "file": "base64:iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAAEAAAABCAYAAAAfFcSJAAAADUlEQVR42mNk+M9QDwADhgGAWjR9awAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==",
  "name": "photo.jpg"
}

Result

  "detected": {
    "ext": "png",
    "mime": "image/png"
  },
  "mismatch": true
}
ai_html_to_markdownHTML to Markdown

Convert HTML to clean Markdown for reading or feeding to a model: headings, lists, links, images, code blocks, GitHub-style tables and strikethrough. Give the HTML inline ("html") or as a file ("path": local path or base64). Optional "readable" keeps only the main article (Mozilla Readability, falls back to the whole body), "selector" converts only matching elements, "baseUrl" makes relative links absolute, "includeLinks": false drops link URLs and images. Scripts, styles and forms are removed. Inline Markdown is cut at maxChars (default 200k) with a note; give "output" to save the whole thing to a .md file. Runs locally; nothing is uploaded.

InputTypeDescription

pathstringAn .html file instead of "html": local path or base64.
readableboolean
default false		Keep only the main content (article text), dropping navigation, ads and footers.
selectorstringCSS selector: convert only the matching elements (e.g. "main", "article .post").
baseUrlstringURL the HTML came from, to make relative links and images absolute.
includeLinksboolean
default true		Keep links and images. False turns links into plain text and drops images.

  "path": "page.html",
  "readable": true,
  "output": "page.md"

ai_image_diffCompare two images

Compare two images pixel by pixel (pixelmatch, anti-aliasing aware) and write a diff PNG with changed pixels in red over a faded copy of the first image. Returns the number and percentage of different pixels. Images must be the same size unless resize is true (the second is stretched to the first's size). Reads PNG, JPEG, WebP, AVIF, GIF (first frame), TIFF and SVG; file path or base64; up to 50 MP. Good for visual regression checks.

InputTypeDescription
a requiredstringThe first (reference) image: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
b requiredstringThe second image: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
thresholdnumber
default 0.1		Colour difference tolerance 0..1; smaller is stricter. Default 0.1.
includeAAboolean
default false		Count anti-aliased edge pixels as differences too. Default false.
resizeboolean
default false		Resize the second image to the first one's size when they differ (otherwise that is an error).
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example: Writes before-diff.png and returns the changed pixel count.

{
  "a": "before.png",
  "b": "after.png",
  "threshold": 0.1
}
ai_json_schema_inferInfer a JSON Schema from samples

Generate a JSON Schema (draft 2020-12) from one or more sample JSON values: types (integer vs number, mixed types as a type list), "null" added where a value can be null, required = keys present in every sample object, array items merged across all elements, string formats guessed when every sample matches (date-time, date, email, uri, uuid), and optional enums for small repeated string sets. Give "samples" (an array of sample values), or "text" (JSON, or JSONL = one sample per line), or "file" (path or base64 of a .json/.jsonl file). An array sample describes an array; to treat its elements as separate samples set eachItem. Pure local code; optionally writes the schema to "output".

InputTypeDescription
samplesobject[]Sample values; each element is one sample.
textstringOr: JSON text (one sample) or JSONL (one sample per line).
filestringOr: a .json/.jsonl file (local path or base64).
eachItemboolean
default false		If the only sample is an array, use its elements as the samples (schema of one record). Default false.
titlestringOptional "title" for the schema.
enumsboolean
default false		Emit "enum" for string fields with few distinct values that repeat. Default false.
maxEnumValuesinteger
default 8		Largest string set turned into an enum (default 8).
formatsboolean
default true		Guess string formats (default true).
requiredboolean
default true		List keys present in every sample as "required" (default true).
additionalPropertiesboolean
default true		false adds additionalProperties: false to every object (strict). Default true (left open).
outputstringOptional: also write the schema as a .json file here.

Example

{
  "samples": [
    {
      "id": 1,
      "email": "a@b.co",
      "tags": [
        "x"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": 2,
      "email": "c@d.org",
      "tags": [],
      "note": null
    }
  ]
}

Result

{
  "samples": 2,
  "schema": {
    "$schema": "https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema",
    "type": "object",
    "properties": {
      "id": {
        "type": "integer"
      },
      "email": {
        "type": "string",
        "format": "email"
      },
      "tags": {
        "type": "array",
        "items": {
          "type": "string"
        }
      },
      "note": {
        "type": "null"
      }
    },
    "required": [
      "id",
      "email",
      "tags"
    ]
  }
}
ai_json_schema_validateValidate JSON against a JSON Schema

Validate JSON data against a JSON Schema with Ajv, reporting every error (allErrors) with its instancePath (JSON Pointer), schemaPath, keyword, message and params. Draft 2020-12 is used when the schema's $schema says 2020-12 (2019-09 likewise), otherwise draft-07 (draft-04/06 schemas are checked as draft-07). Formats checked: date, time, date-time, email, hostname, ipv4, ipv6, uri, uri-reference, uuid, regex, json-pointer (other formats are ignored and listed). Data: inline "data" (any JSON value), "dataText" (JSON text) or "dataFile" (path or base64). Schema: inline "schema" (object or JSON text) or "schemaFile". Remote $ref URLs are never fetched. Runs locally.

InputTypeDescription
dataobjectThe JSON value to validate, inline (a string here is validated as a string; use dataText for JSON text).
dataTextstringOr: the data as JSON text.
dataFilestringOr: a JSON file (local path or base64).
schemaobject | boolean | stringThe JSON Schema inline: an object (or JSON text).
schemaFilestringOr: a JSON Schema file (local path or base64).
maxErrorsinteger
default 100		Most errors to return (default 100).

Example

{
  "data": {
    "name": "Omar",
    "age": -1
  },
  "schema": {
    "type": "object",
    "required": [
      "name",
      "email"
    ],
    "properties": {
      "age": {
        "type": "integer",
        "minimum": 0
      }
    }
  }
}

Result

{
  "valid": false,
  "errorCount": 2,
  "draft": "draft-07"
}

ai_markdown_to_pdfMarkdown to PDF

Turn Markdown (GitHub-flavoured) into a clean, paginated A4/Letter/A5 PDF: headings, paragraphs with word wrap, bold/italic/strikethrough, inline code, links (clickable), bullet / numbered / task lists (nested), tables (wrapped cells, header repeated on new pages), blockquotes, code blocks, horizontal rules, local images (relative to the Markdown file; remote images are never fetched and show as alt text), page breaks (\pagebreak, \newpage or <!-- pagebreak -->), page numbers, margins and title metadata. Text uses the built-in PDF fonts: Latin text only (English and Western European). Arabic, CJK and other scripts are not supported yet: such characters are replaced with "?" (or rejected with onUnsupportedChar: "error"); emoji are dropped; arrows and common symbols are transliterated. Give "markdown" inline or "path" to a .md file.

InputTypeDescription
markdownstringMarkdown text. Use this or "path".
pathstringA .md file: local path or base64. Relative image links resolve against its folder.
titlestringPDF title metadata. Default: the first # / ## heading.
authorstring
paper"A4" | "Letter" | "A5"
default "A4"
landscapeboolean
default false
marginnumber
default 20		Page margin in mm. Default 20.
fontSizenumber
default 10.5		Body font size in pt. Default 10.5.
font"sans" | "serif"
default "sans"		Body font: sans (Helvetica) or serif (Times). Code is always Courier.
pageNumbersboolean
default true		Print "n / total" at the bottom of each page. Default true.
onUnsupportedChar"replace" | "error"
default "replace"		What to do with characters the PDF fonts can't show: replace with "?" (default) or fail.
outputstringWhere to write the result: a file path, or a folder ending in "/". Default: the server output folder (a temp folder unless --out-dir is set).
returnBase64boolean
default false		Also return the output as base64 in the result (only for small files). Default false.

Example

{
  "markdown": "# Report\n\nHello **world**.\n\n- one\n- two\n\n| a | b |\n|---|---|\n| 1 | 2 |",
  "paper": "A4"
}

Result

{
  "pages": 1,
  "title": "Report"
}

Example: Converts a Markdown file; images next to it are embedded.

{
  "path": "notes.md",
  "paper": "Letter",
  "margin": 15
}

ai_ocrOCR (image or scanned PDF to text)

Read the text in an image (PNG, JPEG, WebP, TIFF incl. multi-page, GIF, AVIF) or a scanned PDF with Tesseract (tesseract.js), fully offline: the English and Arabic models ship with the server and nothing is downloaded or uploaded. Languages: "eng", "ara" or "eng+ara" (default). PDF pages are rendered at 200 dpi by default. Returns the text per page and the mean word confidence (0-100); give "output" to also write a .txt file. Limits: up to 200 pages per call, roughly 1-5 s per page; printed text works well, handwriting and very small or skewed text poorly. For PDFs that already have a text layer, pdf_to_text is faster and exact. Give "file" as a local path or base64.

InputTypeDescription
file requiredstringThe image or PDF: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
languages"eng" | "ara" | "eng+ara"
default "eng+ara"		Languages to read. Default "eng+ara".
pagesstringPDF pages / TIFF frames, e.g. "1-3, 5" (default all).
dpiinteger
default 200		Render resolution for PDF pages. Default 200.
psm"auto" | "block" | "line" | "word" | "sparse"
default "auto"		Page layout: "auto" (default), "block" (one uniform block), "line", "word", or "sparse" (scattered text).
perPageboolean
default false		Also return the text of each page separately in "pages". Default false (the joined "text" has page separators).
outputstringAlso write the text to this .txt file (or folder ending in "/").
maxCharsinteger
default 200000		Cut the inline text after this many characters (the file is never cut).

Example

{
  "file": "scan.png",
  "languages": "eng"
}

Example

{
  "file": "scanned-contract.pdf",
  "pages": "1-3",
  "output": "contract.txt"
}

default true		Include speaker notes. Default true.
maxCharsinteger
default 200000		Cut the inline Markdown after this many characters, with a note (a file written with "output" is never cut). Default 200000.
outputstringWrite the Markdown to this file (or folder ending in "/") and return its path instead of the inline text.

Example

{
  "file": "pitch.pptx"
}

Example

{
  "file": "lecture.pptx",
  "slides": "1-10",
  "notes": false
}
ai_sql_querySQL query over tables

Run one read-only SQL SELECT (alasql dialect: JOIN, GROUP BY, HAVING, ORDER BY, LIMIT, UNION, WITH, subqueries, CASE, SUM/COUNT/AVG/MIN/MAX/MEDIAN/STDEV, string and date functions) over one or more tables. Each table is { name, file | csv | json }: a local path or base64 of a CSV, TSV, JSON array, JSONL or .xlsx (first sheet, or "sheet"), or inline CSV text / JSON rows. CSV cells are typed (numbers, true/false, empty → null; ISO dates stay ISO text, so they sort and compare correctly; YEAR()/MONTH() work on them). Only SELECT is allowed: INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/DROP/CREATE/ALTER/ATTACH, SELECT INTO, file readers like FROM CSV(...), inline JavaScript and the -> operator are refused. Table names are case-sensitive; quote odd column names and reserved words (total, value, count, date…) as [My Column]. Returns columns and up to maxRows rows (default 500) with the total count; set "output" (.csv, .tsv, .json, .jsonl or .xlsx) to save every row to a file. Runs locally; nothing is uploaded.

InputTypeDescription
query requiredstringOne SELECT statement, e.g. SELECT region, SUM(amount) AS revenue FROM sales GROUP BY region ORDER BY revenue DESC
tables requiredobject[]The tables the query can read (1-20).
typesboolean
default true		Type CSV cells (numbers, booleans, null). Default true; false keeps every CSV cell as text.
maxRowsinteger
default 500		Rows to return inline (default 500). "total" always counts all rows.
outputstringOptional: save ALL result rows to this file; the type comes from the extension (.csv, .tsv, .json, .jsonl, .xlsx; default .csv).

Example

{
  "query": "SELECT region, SUM(amount) AS revenue FROM sales GROUP BY region ORDER BY revenue DESC",
  "tables": [
    {
      "name": "sales",
      "csv": "region,amount\nNorth,10\nSouth,5\nNorth,7.5"
    }
  ]
}

Result

{
  "columns": [
    "region",
    "revenue"
  ],
  "rows": [
    {
      "region": "North",
      "revenue": 17.5
    },
    {
      "region": "South",
      "revenue": 5
    }
  ],
  "total": 2,
  "truncated": false
}
ai_text_similarityText similarity and near-duplicates

Compare two or more texts (or text files) for overlap. For every pair returns: Jaccard similarity of word shingles (k=3 words by default), a MinHash estimate of that Jaccard (128 hashes), and cosine similarity of word frequencies. Words are lower-cased, punctuation and Arabic diacritics ignored. Measures wording overlap, not meaning (no embeddings): paraphrases score low. Mode "pairs" (default) lists pairs sorted by the chosen metric; mode "dedupe" groups near-duplicates whose metric is at least "threshold" (default 0.8; groups are connected, so A~B and B~C put A, B, C together). Give "texts" (inline strings) and/or "paths" (file paths or base64). Up to 1000 texts.

InputTypeDescription
textsstring[]Texts to compare (inline).
pathsstring[]Or/also: UTF-8 text files (local paths or base64).
labelsstring[]Optional names for the texts, in order (texts first, then paths). Default: "#1", "#2"… or the file name.
mode"pairs" | "dedupe"
default "pairs"		"pairs" (similarity of each pair) or "dedupe" (groups of near-duplicates).
metric"jaccard" | "minhash" | "cosine"
default "jaccard"		Metric used to sort pairs and to group duplicates. Default jaccard.
thresholdnumber
default 0.8		Dedupe: pairs at or above this are duplicates. Default 0.8.
shingleSizeinteger
default 3		Words per shingle (k). Default 3.
numHashesinteger
default 128		MinHash signature length. Default 128 (error about ±0.09).
maxPairsinteger
default 200		Most pairs to return (highest first). Default 200.

Example: Pair #1/#2 scores 1 on every metric.

{
  "texts": [
    "the cat sat on the mat",
    "the cat sat on the mat",
    "a completely different sentence here"
  ]
}

Result

{
  "count": 3,
  "texts": [
    {
      "index": 0,
      "label": "#1",
      "words": 6,
      "shingles": 4
    },
    {
      "index": 1,
      "label": "#2",
      "words": 6,
      "shingles": 4
    },
    {
      "index": 2,
      "label": "#3",
      "words": 5,
      "shingles": 3
    }
  ],
  "pairCount": 3,
  "pairs": [
    {
      "a": 0,
      "b": 1,
      "jaccard": 1,
      "minhash": 1,
      "cosine": 1,
      "labelA": "#1",
      "labelB": "#2"
    },
    {
      "a": 0,
      "b": 2,
      "jaccard": 0,
      "minhash": 0,
      "cosine": 0,
      "labelA": "#1",
      "labelB": "#3"
    },
    {
      "a": 1,
      "b": 2,
      "jaccard": 0,
      "minhash": 0,
      "cosine": 0,
      "labelA": "#2",
      "labelB": "#3"
    }
  ]
}

Example: One group: #1 and #2.

{
  "texts": [
    "Hello world, this is a test.",
    "hello world this is a test",
    "Something else entirely."
  ],
  "mode": "dedupe"
}

Result

{
  "count": 3,
  "texts": [
    {
      "index": 0,
      "label": "#1",
      "words": 6,
      "shingles": 4
    },
    {
      "index": 1,
      "label": "#2",
      "words": 6,
      "shingles": 4
    },
    {
      "index": 2,
      "label": "#3",
      "words": 3,
      "shingles": 1
    }
  ],
  "pairCount": 3,
  "groups": [
    {
      "members": [
        0,
        1
      ],
      "labels": [
        "#1",
        "#2"
      ],
      "keep": 0,
      "minSimilarity": 1
    }
  ],
  "duplicateCount": 1,
  "uniqueCount": 2,
  "pairs": [
    {
      "a": 0,
      "b": 1,
      "jaccard": 1,
      "minhash": 1,
      "cosine": 1,
      "labelA": "#1",
      "labelB": "#2"
    }
  ]
}
ai_token_countCount tokens

Count LLM tokens in a text or text file with the OpenAI tiktoken tokenizers ("o200k_base", default, used by GPT-4o and the o-series; or "cl100k_base", used by GPT-4/3.5 and text-embedding-3). Also returns characters and words, and whether the text fits in common context windows (8k, 32k, 128k, 200k, 1M: e.g. "fits in 200k: yes"). Claude's tokenizer is not public, so for Claude the count is an estimate (typically within ~10-20%; Claude usually needs a little more), returned as a rough range. Runs locally, no API calls. Give "text" or "path" (file path or base64). Large texts (many MB) take a few seconds.

InputTypeDescription
textstringThe text (inline).
pathstringOr: a UTF-8 text/markdown file, as a local path or base64 (raw or a data: URI).
encoding"o200k_base" | "cl100k_base"
default "o200k_base"		Tokenizer: "o200k_base" (GPT-4o / o-series, default) or "cl100k_base" (GPT-4 / GPT-3.5 / text-embedding-3).

Example

{
  "text": "hello world"
}

Result

{
  "tokens": 2,
  "words": 2,
  "characters": 11
}

Example

{
  "path": "notes.md",
  "encoding": "cl100k_base"
}
ai_web_fetchFetch a web page as Markdown

Fetch a web page (http/https) and return its readable content as clean Markdown, with title, byline, final URL after redirects and a word count. Uses Mozilla Readability to keep the main article (falls back to the whole page), or a CSS "selector" you give. Respects robots.txt (refuses pages disallowed for the s3eidat-tools-mcp user agent), only reads HTML and text responses, stops at 5 MB and 20 seconds, follows up to 5 redirects, and refuses private, local and loopback addresses unless allowPrivate is true. Does not run JavaScript, so pages that build their content in the browser may come back nearly empty. Inline Markdown is cut at maxChars (default 200k); "output" saves it to a file.

InputTypeDescription
url requiredstringThe page to fetch (http:// or https://).
selectorstringCSS selector: convert only the matching elements instead of the Readability main content.
readableboolean
default true		Keep only the main content (default true). False converts the whole page body.
includeLinksboolean
default true		Keep links (made absolute) and images. False turns links into plain text and drops images.
allowPrivateboolean
default false		Allow private, local and loopback hosts (e.g. a dev server on localhost). Default false.
maxCharsinteger
default 200000		Cut the inline Markdown after this many characters, with a note (a file written with "output" is never cut). Default 200000.
outputstringWrite the Markdown to this file (or folder ending in "/") and return its path instead of the inline text.

Example

{
  "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Markdown"
}

Example

{
  "url": "https://example.com/docs/page",
  "selector": "main",
  "includeLinks": false
}
ai_xlsx_to_markdownExcel (.xlsx/.csv) to Markdown tables

Turn an Excel .xlsx workbook (or a .csv/.tsv file) into Markdown tables, one "## sheet" section per sheet, or only the sheet you name. The first used row is the table header; empty rows and columns around the data are dropped. Formulas show their cached result (as last saved by Excel), dates are ISO (2026-10-30, or 2026-10-30T14:05:00), merged cells show their value once, hyperlinks become Markdown links. Each sheet shows at most maxRows data rows (default 200) and says how many were left out. Old binary .xls is not supported. Accepts a local path or base64. Runs locally.

InputTypeDescription
file requiredstringThe .xlsx or .csv file: a local file path (absolute, or relative to the server's --root) or base64 data (raw or a data: URI).
sheetstringOnly this sheet: its name, or its 1-based position (e.g. "2"). Default: every sheet.
maxRowsinteger
default 200		Data rows to show per sheet (after the header). Default 200.
format"auto" | "xlsx" | "csv"
default "auto"		Input format. "auto" tells .xlsx (a ZIP) from CSV by its bytes.
maxCharsinteger
default 200000		Cut the inline Markdown after this many characters, with a note (a file written with "output" is never cut). Default 200000.
outputstringWrite the Markdown to this file (or folder ending in "/") and return its path instead of the inline text.

Example

{
  "file": "budget.xlsx"
}

Example

{
  "file": "sales.xlsx",
  "sheet": "Q3",
  "maxRows": 50
}

Example

{
  "file": "contacts.csv"
}
ai_zipZIP create / extract / list

Work with ZIP archives locally. action "create": zip files and folders (given as paths; folders keep their own name as the top folder, symlinks are skipped) into one .zip. action "extract": unpack a .zip (path or base64) into the "output" folder; entries with "..", absolute paths or drive letters are refused (zip-slip protection), existing files are kept unless overwrite is true. action "list": entry names with sizes, no extraction. Limits: total uncompressed size 500 MB and 10000 files by default (maxTotalMB / maxFiles). Encrypted ZIPs are not supported.

InputTypeDescription
action required"create" | "extract" | "list"
filesstring[]create: files and folders to add (local paths).
zipstringextract / list: the ZIP archive, a local path or base64.
outputstringcreate: the .zip path (or a folder ending in "/"). extract: the folder to extract into. Default: the server output folder.
levelinteger
default 6		create: compression level 0 (store) to 9 (smallest). Default 6.
overwriteboolean
default false		extract: replace files that already exist in the output folder. Default false (they are skipped).
maxTotalMBnumber
default 500		Cap on the total uncompressed size, MB. Default 500.
maxFilesinteger
default 10000		Cap on the number of entries. Default 10000.

Example

{
  "action": "create",
  "files": [
    "/projects/site",
    "/notes/readme.txt"
  ],
  "output": "/backups/site.zip"
}

Example

{
  "action": "list",
  "zip": "/downloads/archive.zip"
}

Example

{
  "action": "extract",
  "zip": "/downloads/archive.zip",
  "output": "/downloads/archive/"
}
